- **During the trial, the firmware refuses** configuration saves, updates and the other-slot button (HTTP 409: `testing <version>: N s left`). The trial is short, so the operator waits. A factory reset stays allowed. A configuration save does not confirm the image any more (the rule of #99 is removed).

**The expected mode (item 2).** One rule applies to a rejected configuration:

- An image installed from **normal mode** must reach normal mode. If it rejects the configuration, the firmware rejects the image at once, with the JSON path (`configuration rejected: <path>`). Thus a fleet with nobody near it keeps its MQTT connection.
- An image installed from **recovery mode**, from safe mode, from commissioning, or with **force** (item 3) must reach recovery mode or better. Then it stays, and the operator corrects the configuration in the web UI.
- The firmware stores the expected mode in `fw_last` before `esp_ota_set_boot_partition()`. An image that boots on trial without a record of its own update (for example an installer from before #144) must reach recovery mode or better.

**The pre-flight check (item 3, and #148).** The check runs before the firmware writes past the header of the image (the first 320 B of an upload, the first KB of a download, or the head of the other slot for the button):

- Each configuration key and enum value has a "since" schema number in the validator (`gw_config.c`, table `k_since`; all keys and values of today are schema 1). When the firmware loads or saves the configuration, it calculates the schema that the document needs (`gw_cfgschema`).
- Each image declares in its capabilities block (version 2, after its `esp_app_desc_t`, §"Images that would lose the encrypted NVS" below): the highest schema that it reads (`GW_CONFIG_SCHEMA`), its NVS format and its LittleFS disk version. The NVS format has the page format of the IDF NVS in the high byte (0xFF minus `NVS_CONST_NVS_VERSION`) and the format of the NVS keys and records of the gateway (`GW_NVS_RECORDS`) in the low byte. The build reads `NVS_CONST_NVS_VERSION` and `LFS_DISK_VERSION` from the sources of the libraries (`gw_fw/CMakeLists.txt`), so a library update changes the block too.
- The firmware refuses the image (HTTP 409 for an upload and the button, `failed: …` in the state) when:
  - the stored configuration needs a higher schema than the image reads. The refusal lists the JSON paths that are too new, for example `the configuration needs schema 2, the image reads 1: devices[0].tags[3].scan=slow (2)`;
  - the image has a lower NVS format (either byte) than the running image;
  - the image has another major LittleFS disk version, or a lower minor version.
- An image without capabilities version 2 (all images before #144) is "unknown": the firmware refuses it too, and the web UI says that force is necessary.
- The web UI ("Force" check box) and the API (`"force": true` in the JSON, `?force=1` with an image or with `/api/firmware/other`) permit **force**. Then the expected mode is recovery (item 2). The NCMD has no force. Force does not override the NVS encryption check of #102.
- CAUTION: Force an image with a lower NVS format or LittleFS version only when you accept the loss of the NVS or of `/cfg`. Such an image cannot read the data, and it can erase it.
- When the running image cannot load the stored configuration (recovery mode, safe mode, commissioning), the schema part of the check has nothing to compare and passes. The image must then reach recovery mode or better.
- This fixes the manual downgrade bug of `main` 297c081: a downgrade is refused with the list of keys, or forced into recovery mode, where the operator corrects the configuration.

**The boot-loop counter and safe mode (item 4).** The counter is in RTC memory that a reset does not clear (`RTC_NOINIT`, with a magic number and a CRC). The firmware updates it at the start of `app_main`, before `gw_storage_init()`:

| Reset reason | Counter |
|---|---|
| Panic, interrupt watchdog, task watchdog, other watchdog, CPU lock-up | +1 |
| Power-on, brownout, power glitch, a bad magic number or CRC | 0 |
| Software (a planned restart), the reset button, other reasons | No change |

- A pending image ignores the counter: its trial protects it.
- The counter goes to 0 after 120 s of uptime in normal mode (`CONFIG_GW_FW_BOOTLOOP_CLEAR_S`), and at a restart that the operator asks for (shell, web, NCMD Reboot, a configuration save).
- At 3, the gateway boots in **safe mode**: recovery mode with the cause "repeated crashes". The network, the web UI (also with `web.enabled: false`), the shell and the firmware upload run. Modbus, Sparkplug, Modbus TCP, the spill to the card and the NVS migration do not run. The firmware does not load the configuration. The recovery network uses `lkg_net` (item 8).
- Safe mode ends after 60 min (`CONFIG_GW_FW_SAFE_MODE_MIN`) with a restart that tries normal mode again: the counter becomes 2, so one more crash starts safe mode again. A restart by the operator or a power cycle also ends safe mode.
- There is no automatic boot of the other slot: it could do a downgrade across data formats that nobody examined.
- The shell (`bootloop` line) and `GET /api/firmware` (`boot_count`, `safe_mode`) show the counter. Issue #149 must reserve the RTC memory of the counter in the bootloader before the first flash of a board.

**The last result (item 5).** One NVS string, `fw_last` (namespace `gw`, at most 95 characters), keeps the result of the last update, its cause, the expected mode and the version: `r=<result>;m=<n|r>;v=<version>;c=<cause>`. It is never a raw struct: every image reads it, and a reader ignores the keys that it does not know. The firmware writes it before each step that can end in a restart (the boot partition of an update, the rejection of a new image), and with each other result. Facts that cross images come from this record, the reset reason and `otadata`. RTC memory keeps only the boot-loop counter of the running image, because its address can change between images. Every NBIRTH publishes the result in `Firmware Update/State`. The web UI and the shell show it.

**The other slot (item 6).** The web UI shows both slots: the running one, and the other one with its version and its state (`previous`, `set to boot`, `rejected` with its cause, `invalid`, or `empty`).

- The button "Restart into `<version>`", `POST /api/firmware/other` and the NCMD `Node Control/Boot Other Slot` do the pre-flight check, then `esp_ota_set_boot_partition()` (it verifies the image) in the `fw_update` task, and a planned restart. The other image boots on trial, also when its trial failed before: `esp_ota_set_boot_partition()` writes a new `otadata` entry over an aborted one.
- The firmware refuses it during a trial or a transfer (409), and when the other slot is empty or failed its verification in this boot (404).
- The rejection cause stays in `fw_last`, because `esp_ota_get_last_invalid_partition()` forgets it when `otadata` changes.

**State (item 7).** `Firmware Update/State` (String, alias 41) and `Firmware Update/Progress` (UInt32 %, alias 42). They are in the NBIRTH, and in NDATA when they change (every 10 %). The shell (`status`, line `ota`) and `GET /api/firmware` also give the details.

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `idle` | No update is recorded for the running image |
| `downloading`, `downloading <v>` | A transfer (download or upload) is in progress |
| `restarting into <v>` | The image is set to boot (or the new image was rejected): restart |
| `testing <v>` | The new image runs on trial |
| `installed <v>` | The self-check confirmed the new image. It stays after later restarts |
| `failed: <cause>` | The last update did not take effect: a refused request, a transfer or verification failure, an interrupted update |
| `rolled back from <v>: <cause>` | The earlier image runs again |

The causes of a rollback are: `restarted during the test (<reset reason>)`, `self-check failed: <item>`, `configuration rejected: <path>` and `no memory to start`. The items of the self-check are `mode not reached`, `network driver not started`, `web server not alive` and `main task blocked`.

**The recovery network (item 8).** Recovery mode, safe mode and commissioning start the network with the network of the last valid configuration (`lkg_net` in NVS). The firmware writes it only from a confirmed image in normal mode, after the confirmation. The format tolerates new fields: `GWLN`, a version, then one record for each field (tag, length, value). A reader takes the tags that it knows and ignores the others. A field that is not there keeps its default (DHCP). Thus a rollback into recovery mode keeps the static IP of a site. The raw struct of the firmware before #144 is not read: no gateway is installed yet, so the first boot of this firmware starts with DHCP until a valid configuration writes the new record.

**Manual rollback.** The developers test a release so that it does not break MQTT. If MQTT does not work after an update because of the setup of the site or of the broker, the operator does a manual rollback: upload the earlier image, or use the button "Restart into `<version>`".

**Accepted risk.** An image that passes the self-check, but cannot connect to the broker of a site, stays installed until a person uses the web UI (manual rollback). For fleets, update a pilot gateway first, and then update the others in stages.

**Last resort.** If the web UI is not reachable in any mode, flash the firmware over USB (§3.2). The owner of the board always keeps this access (no Secure Boot, no flash encryption, §3.7).

**Images that would lose the encrypted NVS (issue #102).** Each image declares its capabilities in a 32 B block immediately after its `esp_app_desc_t` (section `.rodata_custom_desc`, offset 288 in the image). The block holds `GWCP`, the version, the size and two flags: *encrypted NVS* (`CONFIG_NVS_ENCRYPTION`) and *encrypted flash* (only for information; no build in `main` sets it, §3.7). Version 2 (#144) adds the schema, the NVS format and the LittleFS disk version. A gateway can have an encrypted NVS (or a pending migration). On such a gateway, the firmware rejects an image that does not declare encrypted NVS, also with force. That image could not read the NVS and would erase it (bdSeq, secrets), and a later *rollback* would also find it erased.

The rejection occurs as soon as the header arrives (the first 320 B of an upload, or the first KB of a download, read again from the partition). This is much before `esp_ota_set_boot_partition`: `failed: image <v> refused: built without NVS encryption (this device's NVS is encrypted)`. An image without the block (from before this change) counts as an image that does not declare encrypted NVS. While the NVS stays unencrypted (image on trial, safe mode, no LittleFS), or in the build without encryption, the firmware accepts all images.

### 3.7 eFuse-burning features: archived (FW-2, FW-3, CFG-7)

**Decision of issue #133 (option 3):** the project does not burn eFuses on the board. These features are not in `main`:

- Secure Boot V2 with RSA-3072 (FW-2);
- flash encryption in *release* mode (FW-3);
- NVS encryption with an HMAC key in the eFuse block KEY5 on the board (the eFuse part of CFG-7);
- the serial download mode disabled permanently (`CONFIG_SECURE_DISABLE_ROM_DL_MODE`).

**The reason.** This is an open-source project for a board that anyone can buy. Each of these features burns eFuses at the first boot, and an eFuse burn is permanent. After the burn, only the person with the signing key can change the firmware. The owner of the board cannot read or flash the board through USB. This is a vendor lock, and the project does not lock the devices of their owners.

**The archive branch.** The branch `archive/efuse-production` keeps these features as they were in `main` at commit 4a0e01c. It contains:

- the overlay `sdkconfig.production` and the partition table `partitions-production.csv`;
- `tools/sign_firmware.sh` (the offline signature and its checks), `tools/check_production.sh` and `tools/e2e/secure_boot_qemu.sh`;
- the CI job `firmware-production`;
- the build checks of the production options (issues #93 and #108 B7) and their host tests;
- §3.7 and §6.12 of its `idf/README.md`: the factory procedure, the management of the signing key and the known limitations.

> **Warning: Do not flash a build of the archive branch on a board that you want to use again.** Its first boot burns eFuses, and you cannot undo this. Nobody tested that branch on a real board, and the project does not maintain it.

**The default build burns no eFuse.** This was examined on the default build of this change (`idf.py set-target esp32s3 build`, IDF v6.1):

- The `sdkconfig` has no option that burns an eFuse. These options are off: `CONFIG_SECURE_BOOT`, `CONFIG_SECURE_FLASH_ENC_ENABLED`, `CONFIG_SECURE_DISABLE_ROM_DL_MODE`, `CONFIG_SECURE_ENABLE_SECURE_ROM_DL_MODE`, `CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_APP_ANTI_ROLLBACK` and `CONFIG_NVS_ENCRYPTION`.
- The ROM log mode stays `CONFIG_BOOT_ROM_LOG_ALWAYS_ON` (the IDF default). The other ROM log modes burn an eFuse at the first boot.
- `BOOTLOADER_APP_ROLLBACK_ENABLE` (FW-1) uses only `otadata`. It is not the *anti-rollback*, which burns an eFuse counter.
- The app and the bootloader contain no function that writes eFuses (`esp_efuse_write_*`, `esp_efuse_utility_burn_*`, `efuse_hal_program`). `xtensa-esp32s3-elf-nm` finds only read functions, for example the MAC address and the chip version.

**The QEMU build** (`sdkconfig.qemu`) still has two features that issue #133 must decide. Each one uses the emulated eFuse file of the run, and nothing real is burned:

- **Signed OTA images without Secure Boot** (`CONFIG_SECURE_SIGNED_APPS_NO_SECURE_BOOT`). `esp_ota_end()` verifies the RSA-3072 signature of each image with the public key of the running app. This burns no eFuse, also on a board. `tools/e2e/run_e2e.sh` generates the test key in `build-qemu/`.
- **NVS encryption** with the HMAC key in KEY5, and the migration from an unencrypted NVS (§3.5, issues #67 and #94).

Until #133 decides, the project adds no work that depends on these two features.

> **Caution: Never flash the QEMU build on a board.** On a real chip, its first boot burns the HMAC key into KEY5 for the NVS encryption, and you cannot undo this. Also, the QEMU build simulates the I/O.

The measurements of the production build in §3.2 are from before the archive.

## 4. Verification (real evidence)

### 4.1 QA-1: host tests (CMake + Unity v2.6.1 + CTest)

```bash
cmake -S idf/test/host -B build-host && cmake --build build-host -j3 && ctest --test-dir build-host
```

Result: **45/45 CTest tests (27 Unity suites, `test_sfcfg_packed` and `sfcfg_guard` from issue #145, `check_set_config` from issue #58 and the 15 `sdkconfig_check_*` from #83, #93, #103 and #108) and 288/288 cases OK** (issue #145 added `test_sfcfg`, 5 cases, which `test_sfcfg_packed` runs again with another memory layout; issue #136 added 3 cases to `test_sf`; issue #139 added `test_cardfmt`, 7 cases; issue #124: `test_sf` had 18 cases, one less, but its fault test runs 376 restarts). The test files compile with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`. The host build of `gw_core` also uses `-Wshadow -Wconversion`.

Each step changes the card a maximum of one time. Six mutations of the code (no nonce check, no CRC, no checkpoint limit, no position rule, the same superblock copy each time, K = 0) each make at least one case fail. Issue #136, the move of the RAM ring before a planned restart: samples below three quarters of the RAM ring (none on the card), the move, a boot: all the samples come back one time, in order, and a second boot sends nothing again; the same with samples on the card and in RAM. Each step is one card operation with a watchdog feed after it, and the last superblock waits for the gap of 500 ms. A slow card (300 ms for each operation, a limit of 500 ms): the move stops in the limit plus one operation, the samples on the card come back in order, and the rest is counted as lost. A torn block write during the move: the move stops at once, and the boot publishes nothing of that block. A failed spill, a boot recovery that did not end, and no spill: no card operation. Four mutations (a block that does not leave the RAM ring, a string sample cut between two blocks, no time limit, the newest samples first) each make at least one case fail |

TCA9554 boot (latch OFF, POLARITY = 0 and then CONFIG, also at a warm boot), DO/3 → low pin and read-back, bus errors, read-back with an inherited inverted POLARITY, retry with backoff. IO-3: the latch of the `hold` outputs is kept only with output pins (warm boot), OFF after a power-on, a failed read without writes. A write of several DOs in one transaction |
| `test_localio` | 7 | IO-3: boot state per output, the safe state after N s without a session (from boot or from the loss, one time, re-arm, 32-bit clock wrap). IO-4: the save schedule of the counters. IO-7: `follow`, `invert`, `toggle` (restored pulses, an even number of activations), the safe state without the rule DOs |
| `test_buzz` | 3 | IO-6: requested beeps, alarms with a delay, priority and a 10 s period, a request before the burst, a silent buzzer |
| `test_rtc` | 4 | TIME-2: BCD, PCF85063 registers ↔ UTC (each day from 2025 to 2099 against the libc), the OS bit, invalid fields (31 November, 29 February, hour 24), out of range. The gateway never saves a time that is not set |
| `test_ota` | 16 | Issue #118: after each kind of rollback decision (rejected configuration, cannot start, timeout), the policy does nothing more, so the restart is the only way out. Issue #108: no save and no factory reset after a rollback decision; a requested restart retries the confirmation only in CONFIRMING. Issue #99: a session without all the DBIRTHs does not confirm (the deadline does a rollback and gives the reason). The confirmation stays pending until the write works, and the gateway retries it at 1 s … 60 s without a deadline (also across the 32-bit clock wrap). Operator confirmation (save, factory reset; it retries immediately). No memory to boot → *rollback*. Issue #101: a rejection does not hide the transfer in progress or the trial. Issue #102: the capability block (magic, version, size, newer version), and when an image can replace the running image. FW-1: the first session confirms a new image on trial (one decision only). *Rollback* by deadline (also with the 32-bit clock wrap) and by a rejected configuration, confirmation in commissioning. URLs (`https://` only, host, characters, credentials, length), the `{"url"}` body from the web, progress every 10 % or every 5 s |
| `test_nvsraw` | 23 | Issue #109, second review: the code recognizes the «erase in progress» entry without the namespace entry (on another page, already erased), with any index, only by its value. The code erases an NVS that does not boot because of its format, without the entry. Issue #107: three damaged strings or blobs in a page of 8 items count as 3 bad entries (not one per entry), and the page stays plaintext; a damaged size counts per entry. Issue #109: plaintext, or mixed with keys, without the «erase in progress» entry → stays plaintext; with the entry → no NVS. Issue #108: a plaintext partition, or one mixed with keys, and the copy out of reach → no NVS. The bdSeq reservation chain (the simulation from the review, 40 boots without NVS: it never goes back), the boot with NVS that continues from the reservation, the one-byte file of #104. Issue #67: unencrypted NVS or encrypted NVS by its raw pages, with images from `nvs_partition_gen.py` (`generate` and `encrypt` of the same CSV). CRC equal to `esp_rom_crc32_le`, blank pages, erased pages or pages with a bad header, one corrupt entry, several pages. Issue #94: plaintext pages and encrypted pages mixed (`MIXED`, in any order; a plaintext page with all its entries damaged counts as encrypted). The bdSeq read from the raw pages (another namespace, an encrypted page, two copies written: the copy with the higher sequence wins, invalid CRC). The migration copy (round trip, one byte changed, short, long, another *magic*, empty, no space, names of 16 characters or empty names). The boot decision for each partition type, copy (valid, damaged or unreadable), image on trial, LittleFS and unreadable pages (nothing readable or both types: no NVS; on trial with a pending copy: no NVS). The read of the copy (current version, version 1 converted, unknown version kept, damaged or without *magic* erased, no space for the conversion). When an erase is permitted (`may_erase`: format error or not, skipped entries, truncated list). The newer bdSeq of two (across 255, symmetric at a distance of 128). Issue #104: the copy out of reach (LittleFS that does not mount and is not blank: empty NVS with keys, or encrypted NVS with the start mark → no NVS; blank, without keys or migration complete → normal). The bdSeq reservation (never below the value already given, for each initial value). Issue #97: FULL and FREEING pages are read as the active page (CORRUPT, INVALID and blank pages are not; the bdSeq is also read from a full page; a full plaintext page with the active page encrypted gives `MIXED`). More bad entries than good entries (the rule `plain_ok >= bad` stays, see §6.2.1: a tie is plaintext; one more bad entry is read as encrypted and is not migrated). The clip of the *span* (it ends exactly at the last entry, it goes past it, 0 or 255: it counts 1, and the code reads the next entry) |
| `test_cardfmt` | 7 | Issues #139 and #137: the format requests. A format that runs refuses the next request (busy) before any other reason, also a simulated one. Then a build without a card (no support) and a board without PSRAM. A refusal changes nothing; a new format clears the result of the last one. The emulator test run needs neither a card nor PSRAM. The time of a running format across a wrap of the clock, and the fixed duration after the end. The text and the JSON of each phase (idle, running, done with the restart prompt, failed): a reason with quotes, a backslash and a control character stays valid JSON, a buffer that is too small gives `{}`, a long reason is cut at its field. The host tests download Unity (v2.6.1) and cJSON (v1.7.19) with `FetchContent` from GitHub. Both are **pinned by commit hash**.

### 4.2 Sparkplug interoperability

```bash
mkdir -p /tmp/spb && build-host/spb_dump /tmp/spb && python3 idf/tools/spb_decode.py --check /tmp/spb
```

`spb_dump` generates real NDEATH, NBIRTH, DBIRTH, DDATA and NDATA payloads from the engine. `spb_decode.py` uses `grpc_tools.protoc` to compile the official `sparkplug_b.proto` of eclipse/tahu (a copy without changes in `components/gw_core/proto/`). Then it decodes the payloads with **Google protobuf 7.36**.

Result: **14 payloads decoded, 0 structural errors**. The payloads include a DBIRTH and a DDATA with UInt64, Int64, Double, String and a register bit, and an NBIRTH with the UInt64 `Counter/n` counters of IO-4. From SPB-16 to SPB-19, they include these items: the properties `engUnit`/`engLow`/`engHigh`/`Quality` (types and codes checked), a DDATA with `is_null` and BAD, a `bytes` tag, the DataSet `Node Info/Modbus` and a DataSet with all column classes, a File, and a node with templates (definition in the NBIRTH, instances in DBIRTH and DDATA). `spb_decode.py --check` also validates the structure of the DataSets (columns, types, one cell per column in its field). It also validates the structure of the templates (definition only in NBIRTH, without alias or `template_ref`; members with name and datatype, without alias). It requires the datatype Int64 (4) for `bdSeq` in the NDEATH and in the NBIRTH (issue #154). The script also decoded the 2065 messages that the E2E captured.

### 4.3 QA-3: end to end with the real firmware in QEMU

```bash
. $IDF_PATH/export.sh
python3 $IDF_PATH/tools/idf_tools.py install qemu-xtensa   # requires libslirp0
python3 -m venv ~/e2e && ~/e2e/bin/pip install -r idf/tools/e2e/requirements.txt
E2E_PYTHON=~/e2e/bin/python idf/tools/e2e/run_e2e.sh
```

On Windows, or without a local ESP-IDF, run the E2E tests in the CI image with `idf/tools/e2e/Dockerfile`. Refer to `docs/how-to/run-e2e-in-docker.md`. `docs/reference/e2e-durations.md` gives the durations on such a computer.

**Test bench:**

- QEMU `esp_develop_9.2.2`, machine `esp32s3`, with the firmware compiled with the `sdkconfig.qemu` overlay. The OTA scenarios (FW-1 OTA, the #97 integration, the trial of #99/#101 and the OTA to encrypted NVS) run with single-threaded TCG (`-accel tcg,thread=single`: the two cores in one host thread; issue #107). With MTTCG, the QEMU default, the emulator sometimes crashes in these scenarios when the host load is high (§6.10, `docs/upstream-reports.md` §7). The other scenarios continue with MTTCG. With one thread, the guest is 1.5 to 2 times slower. Its real-time bounds failed in one full run with one thread, while another run ran in parallel (163/170). These bounds are: DI to NDATA in less than 100 ms, the polling cadence, the probe *backoff*, and a GET in less than 1 s during a rejected upload. `E2E_TCG_THREAD` (or `--tcg-thread`) selects `auto` (the behavior above, the default), `single` or `multi` for all scenarios. For the diagnosis of a QEMU crash, each emulator runs in its own directory `qemu/NNN-<log>/` with core dumps permitted (up to 4 GB; the `core_pattern` of the container is `core`). If the emulator leaves a core dump, the harness uses gdb to write the trace of each thread next to it (`core-bt.txt`; the CI uploads this file, but not the *core*). The stderr file of each boot starts with the command line, the number of other QEMU processes on the host and the load. `results.json` keeps the QEMU version and the `emulators` list (command line, pid, load and host QEMU processes at the start, how it ended, *core* and trace).
- Ethernet `open_eth` on slirp.
- UART1 → socket → **pymodbus 3.8.6** as the RTU slave, with units 1 and 2. Unit 1 (`Meter1`) has the tags of §6, a writable float32 `Limit` and the MB-6 tags: `Energy` uint64 ABCD, `Counter` int64 CDAB, `Flow` float64 DCBA, `Temp` int32 BADC, `Serial` string, and the register bits `Alarm` and `Ready`. Unit 1 has `max_gap: 10`, and `Energy` and `Serial` are in the slow scan of 3 s. Unit 2 (`Bulk`) has 60 registers. Units 3 to 5 (`Dead3`–`Dead5`) are in the configuration but not on the bus: they never respond.
- UART0 → socket, for the log and the shell (the harness injects the DIs with the shell command `di`).
- Modbus TCP (MB-15): slirp forwards a host port (`--mbtcp-port`, default 15502) to port 502 of the gateway (`hostfwd`). A pymodbus client of the harness uses this port. The connections come from 10.0.2.2, the only address in `allowed_clients` in the E2E configuration.
- A private **mosquitto 2.0.18** (a renamed copy of the binary), an SNTP responder of the harness on UDP 123 and a UDP syslog receiver (`--syslog-port`, default 15514). The harness sends signals only to the processes that it started.
- **Brokers with bad behavior:** a minimal MQTT server of the harness on `--mqtt-port` + 1 accepts the connection and cuts it after the CONNACK. On `--mqtt-port` + 2, it responds to each SUBSCRIBE with 0x80. (mosquitto cannot do this: its ACL never rejects a SUBSCRIBE, it only filters at delivery.)
- **Network isolation.** The harness moves itself into its own *network namespace*, with only the loopback interface (this needs `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`, for example root in a container). All ports, also UDP 123, are private to the run, so two parallel runs do not collide. Without this permission (or with `--no-isolate`), the harness uses the host loopback with the ports of `--mqtt-port`, `--modbus-port` and `--console-port` (or `E2E_MQTT_PORT`, `E2E_MODBUS_PORT`, `E2E_CONSOLE_PORT`). The default ports are 18831, 15020 and 15555. `run_e2e.sh` passes its extra arguments to `e2e.py`.
- **A second build without NVS encryption (issue #94).** `run_e2e.sh` also builds `build-qemu-plain` (`sdkconfig.qemu-plain`: the QEMU build with `CONFIG_NVS_ENCRYPTION=n`, without PSRAM in the two runs; `JOBS` sets its parallel jobs). It passes this image with `--plain-flash`: it is the firmware before the OTA of the `GW42` scenario. `E2E_PLAIN=0` skips this build, and the harness reports that scenario as `SKIP`. The broker of this scenario and the broker of `GW41` require a user and a password (`mosquitto_passwd`). Thus the scenario really tests the MQTT password that the NVS keeps.
- The harness writes its own `config.json`, with the selected MQTT port, into the image under test. For the configuration boots, it generates variants of the image with a different `storage` partition (with `littlefs-python`, the same tool that the build uses) or with the partition erased. The harness reads the size limit from `build-qemu/sdkconfig` (`CONFIG_GW_CONFIG_MAX_BYTES`).
- The check of "nothing started" uses a positive signal: the line `nothing is started` and, 6 s later, the response of the shell command `status` (`nothing is running: no polling, no MQTT; recovery network and web UI only`). An additional check makes sure that no boot logs of the RS485 UART and of MQTT occur, and no NBIRTH and no requests to the Modbus slave.
- **TLS (MQTT-4/5):** the harness uses `openssl` to generate its own CA (EC P-256), a broker certificate for `IP:10.0.2.2` and `DNS:broker.e2e`, and a client certificate. It starts a separate mosquitto with four listeners: TLS 1.3 only, TLS 1.2 only with a mandatory client certificate, plain TCP for the observer, and TLS 1.3 with an expired broker certificate (#70, `GW25`). The PEM files go into the LittleFS partition of each variant.
- **Broker list (MQTT-6):** the primary is a port where, at first, nothing listens. Then the harness starts another mosquitto on this port and stops it again. The backup is the main mosquitto.
- **Primary host (SPB-13):** the harness itself publishes the STATE messages (retained Sparkplug 3.0 JSON) of the host `SCADA1`.
- **TCP relay** of the harness between the gateway and the main mosquitto. When the harness closes the relay, the gateway loses the broker, but the observer stays connected (store & forward, SPB-14). When the harness reads the relay at 700 B/s, it simulates a slow link (issue #59).
- HTTPS (issue #64): slirp forwards `127.0.0.1:18443` of the host to port 443 of the gateway (`--https-port`), and `127.0.0.1:18080` to port 80 (`--http-port`). Port 80 only redirects. The harness uses HTTPS without a verification of the certificate (it is self-signed), and compares its fingerprint with the fingerprint on the console. The harness queries the shell with a common helper that asks again until it sees all the expected lines (#56). `--only` runs only some scenarios. The names are `failsafe`, `network`, `web`, `reset`, `tls`, `failover`, `mqtt5`, `phost`, `sf`, `https`, `ota`, `ota-fw1`, `ota-park`, `ota-wiring`, `ota-trial`, `nvs`, `slow`, `refuse` and `main`. `ota` selects the four OTA scenarios: FW-1 OTA (`ota-fw1`), the park of #112 (`ota-park`), the #97 integration (`ota-wiring`) and the trial of #99/#101 (`ota-trial`). The CI uses the four names to put these scenarios into different jobs (§4.5, issue #121). If a name is not known, the harness stops with an error. `refuse` runs as a separate scenario only when `main` is not in the list (`main` includes it). `tools/e2e/scenarios.py` holds the list of the scenarios and their names.

Configuration used: `tools/e2e/fs/config.json` (keepalive of 10 s, syslog to the receiver of the harness). `run_e2e.sh` also passes the ELF and the IDF python, to decode the *core dump* with `esp_coredump`.

Result with issue #145 (branch `fix/spill-canonical-hash`, on `main` 297c081; `tools/e2e/run_ci_groups.sh`, the 5 CI groups): **181/181 checks OK** as one full run (201/201 in the groups) and, with the octal PSRAM, **188/188** (208/208). Both runs had no retries (`retries` and `remeasured` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). `GW23` has one new check in the PSRAM variant (row `GW23` below): a saved configuration that changes only fields outside the hash keeps the data on the card, and a tag name change starts a new ring. The replay checks of #136 now also require that the boot did not start a new ring. The default variant has no spill, so it has no new check. The #80 check passed: without PSRAM, minimum free internal RAM 32 904 B and lowest largest block at a validation 18 432 B; with PSRAM, 160 844 B and 122 880 B (thresholds 8 KB and 17 KB). The block of 18 432 B was the lowest value so far, but #145 adds no `.bss` and no allocation. Three more runs of `--only tls` gave 27 648, 23 552 and 27 648 B on the branch, and 27 648, 25 600 and 27 648 B on `main` 297c081 (minimum free 33 004 to 33 208 B in all six runs). Thus the low value comes from the load of the parallel groups, not from the change. The `sparkplug` stack kept 1 692 B (default) and 1 700 B (PSRAM) free in the deepest paths, the same as before (threshold 1 KB).

Result with issue #136 (branch `fix/flush-before-restart`, on `main` 48311a7; `tools/e2e/run_ci_groups.sh`, the 5 CI groups): **181/181 checks OK** as one full run (201/201 in the groups) and, with the octal PSRAM, **187/187** (207/207). Both runs had no retries (`retries` and `remeasured` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). `GW23` has one new check in the PSRAM variant: a planned restart while offline, with the shell `restart` and with `POST /api/restart`. The move took 794 and 835 ms for 360 and 300 samples (3 and 2 blocks, with the gap of 500 ms before the last superblock), and the `sparkplug` stack kept 2 788 B free at that time. The default variant has no spill, so it has no new check. The #80 check passed: without PSRAM, minimum free internal RAM 33 024 B and lowest largest block at a validation 24 576 B (the same as in the #124 run; 25 600 B in the #139 run); with PSRAM, 157 752 B and 122 880 B (thresholds 8 KB and 17 KB). The `sparkplug` stack kept 1 692 B (default) and 1 700 B (PSRAM) free in the deepest paths, the same as before (threshold 1 KB).

Result with issue #139 (branch `feature/card-format-web`, on `main` 24462f4; `tools/e2e/run_ci_groups.sh`, the 5 CI groups): **181/181 checks OK** as one full run (201/201 in the groups) and, with the octal PSRAM, **186/186** (206/206). Both runs had no retries (`retries` and `remeasured` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). `GW23` has three new checks in each variant: the web API (its refusal and its auth and CSRF checks), the status objects and the page, and the format task with a simulated format. The #80 check passed: without PSRAM, minimum free internal RAM 33 076 B and lowest largest block at a validation 25 600 B; with PSRAM, 159 340 B and 122 880 B (thresholds 8 KB and 17 KB). The `sparkplug` stack kept 1 692 B (default) and 1 700 B (PSRAM) free in the deepest paths, the same as before (threshold 1 KB).

Result with issue #124 (branch `feature/sd-ring-step2`, on `main` 565dbfe; `tools/e2e/run_ci_groups.sh`, the 5 CI groups): **178/178 checks OK** as one full run (198/198 in the groups) and, with the octal PSRAM, **183/183** (203/203). Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). `GW23` has one check without PSRAM (the spill does not start, and `card format confirm` refuses) and five with PSRAM (the spill and its replay, the bounded steps, a power cut while offline, the migration, the format command); before, it had two checks in both variants. The #80 check passed: without PSRAM, minimum free internal RAM 31 912 B and lowest largest block at a validation 24 576 B; with PSRAM, 158 128 B and 122 880 B (thresholds 8 KB and 17 KB). The `sparkplug` stack kept 1 692 B (default) and 1 700 B (PSRAM) free in the deepest paths (threshold 1 KB).

Result with issue #122 (branch `fix/sd-spill-step1`, on `main` 3e6c982; `tools/e2e/run_ci_groups.sh`, the 5 CI groups): **179/179 checks OK** as one full run (199/199 in the groups) and, with the octal PSRAM, **180/180** (200/200). Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). In the PSRAM run, the group `other` first failed in 10 scenarios with `No space left on device` on the host (another work used the disk). Its second run, from the same images, passed 75/75. The new check of `GW23` passed in both variants (row `GW23` below). The #80 check passed: minimum free internal RAM 33 416 B, and lowest largest block at a validation 20 480 B (thresholds 8 KB and 17 KB). Earlier runs of `main` gave 23 552 to 28 672 B for that block. #122 adds no allocation on that path, but its `.bss` is 304 B larger. Examine this value in the next runs.

After the review of #110, the result is **173/173 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM, **174/174**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). The review put the web *watchdog* in its own task, `web_guard`. The watchdog is alive at the entry to and the exit from the *handlers*, and during the discard. `shutdown()` applies only to sockets of the server port. An earlier run without PSRAM, on a machine with load from other runs (load ≈ 8.6), failed in 3 OTA scenarios because of QEMU deaths (#107). The harness did that full run again. Stacks: `web_guard` 924–1 116 B free of 2.5 KB, `httpd` 5 408–8 844 B, `httpd_redirect` 1 096–1 756 B. A `GET` during the *slowloris* got a response in 8.4 s. `.bss` +2 672 B (the static stack and TCB of `web_guard`).

With issue #110 (branch `fix/web-110` on `main` d82ea4a): **173/173 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM, **174/174**. In the run without PSRAM, 2 scenarios had a retry because of known emulator faults (`Bad ram pointer` and a QEMU SIGSEGV, #107: this tree still runs QEMU with multi-threaded TCG). In the run with PSRAM, 1 scenario had a retry (`LoadStorePIFAddrError` on a peripheral during the OTA). The new checks examine the stack of the two `httpd` tasks separately (in `GW29` and `GW22`), and the *slowloris* (`GET` answered in 8.0 s; 30.2 s with the *watchdog* disabled). Without PSRAM, with the usual 4 receive buffers, the `open_eth` model still lost 8 frames in the full run (without failures). With PSRAM and 10 buffers, it lost no frames. The margin of #80 without PSRAM: minimum free internal RAM 36 912 B, largest free block at the validation 27 648 B.

After the review of #106, the result is **167/167 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM, **168/168**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). The review changed these items: the gateway discards authenticated rejections completely, always closes cut sessions, uses an atomic counter, and shows network errors on the page. The new check: an authenticated upload of 1.5 MB from the same origin that is not an image gets `400 Bad Request` after the client sends all of it.

Result with issue #113 (branch `fix/rx-frames`, with `main` fc77020 included; the web clients of the harness retry the TLS *handshake*; 4 receive buffers without PSRAM, as before): **177/177 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM, **178/178**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). `--only tls,https --web-stress 60` without PSRAM gave 60 rounds without failures and without client *timeouts*. The `open_eth` model lost 27 frames, and 0 *handshakes* had a retry. None of those frames was a SYN, so that run did not need the retry. A separate check examined the retry against a server that never completes the *handshake*. The margin of #80 without PSRAM: largest free block at the validation 25 600–27 648 B (threshold 17 KB, no change).

Result with issue #115 (branch `fix/upload-timeout`, on `main` 6555e79; the uploads wait for progress): **177/177 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`), **178/178**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` and `faults` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). With PSRAM, the upload of the flood check got a response after 186 s. The upload of `e2e-v3` went to trial after 161 s. Both stayed far from all limits. A first run measured the progress by the bytes that the client sent, and it failed. slirp accepts the full megabyte immediately, so after that the client only waited 90 s without progress. For this reason, the check uses the progress that the gateway reports.

Result with issue #114 (branch `fix/e2e-psram-timing`, on `main` 59bdbc2, with the OTA scenarios in single-threaded TCG from #107): **177/177 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`), **178/178**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` and `faults` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). With PSRAM, the upload of the flood check got a response after 185 s (before, the `POST` was cut at 150 s). FW-1 OTA took 504 s.

Result with issue #112 (branch `fix/park-followup`, on `main` 03544d0): **174/174 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`), **175/175**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). The new scenario `GW47` has a 5 s timeout, 5 retries and a silent `Meter1`. In both runs, it parked core 1 5.0 s after the start of the transaction, at the end of its first attempt. Two earlier runs without PSRAM gave 173/174, with QEMU deaths (SIGSEGV, #107). The cause was the load of the 16 runs of the reproduction attempt of §8 of `docs/upstream-reports.md`. One time, two deaths occurred in sequence in `sf spill` (this scenario gets only one retry). The other time, SPB-2/SPB-11 failed in the retry of `main` because of a harness defect. That check counts the NDEATH messages with the expected bdSeq in the full run. Probably it also counted the *will* that the broker published when QEMU died in the first attempt. (The retry boots from a new flash and repeats the bdSeq values.) This defect stays as it is.

Result with issue #111 (branch `fix/park-deadlock`, on `main` d82ea4a): **172/172 checks OK**, with one retry because of a known emulator fault. QEMU stopped with `atomic_mmu_lookup: code should not be reached`, signal 6, in FW-1 OTA; the second attempt passed. With the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`): **173/173** without retries (`retries` and `faults` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). The two new checks of #111 passed in both runs. There were 123 and 109 parks of core 1, and the longest took 764 and 496 ms (the limit is 45 s). With a console flood, the rejected upload got a response in 7.8 to 11 s.

Result with issue #106 (branch `fix/web-robust`, with `main` 7681914 included): **166/166 checks OK** without retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). With the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`): **167/167**, with one retry because of a known emulator fault (`LoadStorePIFAddrError` on a peripheral during the write of the image, `FW-1 OTA`). Before, on the same branch, two runs failed because of the emulator, on a machine with load from other runs. One run had a hang at boot after the *panic* of #62, immediately after the start of UART1. The other run had `Bad ram pointer` / QEMU SIGSEGV in the PSRAM variant. Another run with PSRAM failed in the check of the web UI of #100. The third `PUT` (with the header, without `Origin`) did not open a connection in 15 s. The gateway log did not show even the TLS *handshake*, and the next request got a normal response. This problem did not occur again. The detail of the check now shows the client error.

Result after issue #107 (branch `fix/qemu-segv` with `main` d82ea4a, single-threaded TCG in the OTA scenarios, another E2E ran at the same time on the host): **170/170 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM, **171/171**. Both runs had no retries and no emulator faults (`retries` and `faults` empty). Before: **165/165 checks OK** (branch `fix/e2e-harness` with `main` fd2316f included, thus with `fix/web-hardening` and the two rounds of `fix/ota-trial`, after the third review of the harness: issues #92, #95, #96, #97, #104 point 3 and #105; no retry, `retries` and `faults` empty in `results.json`, no `[PANIC]`). With the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`, #85): **166/166**, with one retry. In the scenario of the #97 integration, core 1 had the `LoadStorePIFAddrError` on SYSTIMER (`0x6002304c`) during the verification of the uploaded image (`esp_image: segment` lines immediately before). This is the known signature; the second attempt passed. The time of each scenario (`durations`, without PSRAM / with PSRAM): `main` 201 / 199 s, FW-1 OTA 98 / 107 s, the trial of #99/#101 69 / 87 s, the OTA to encrypted NVS 73 s without PSRAM, the #97 integration 74 s with PSRAM, and less for the other scenarios. The limit of 900 s per attempt is more than four times the longest scenario. With the single-threaded TCG of the OTA scenarios (#107), the longest scenario becomes the #97 integration with PSRAM, 477 s. The limit is then less than two times this value, so on a slower machine, use `E2E_TIMEOUT_SCALE`. Before the second review, on `main` cf6fc7c: 156/156 and 157/157. Before, on `feature/firmware`: **152/152 checks OK** (on `feature/firmware` with `main` a0b4a10 included, after the fixes of issues #93, #94 (with its second and third review), #98 and of point 5 of #99: FW-1/FW-2, #67, #80, #84, #86, #87, #88, #89, #90, #94 and #98; no QEMU retry in that run, `retries` empty in `results.json`). With the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`, #85): **153/153**, also without retries (see «Octal PSRAM» below). An earlier run, with the same code, gave 150/152. QEMU stopped responding in `esp_ota_mark_app_invalid_rollback_and_reboot()` (it did not end, so the harness does not count it as a QEMU death). This occurred after `Take trusted digest key(s) from running app`, at the return from `e2e-v3` when the deadline expired. The same trace occurred on this branch before these changes (§6.10). After the second review of #94, one run gave 151/152. In `GW42`, the image without NVS encryption had a *panic* on core 1 (`LoadStorePIFAddrError` at `0x6002306c`, SYSTIMER). The *panic* occurred during the verification of the image that the gateway downloaded a second time. This is the same type of QEMU fault as the cache freeze of §4.3; the next run passed completely. with `--panics 3`, on `feature/sparkplug-metrics`, the three dumps decoded (#62).
Result of `fix/nvs-edge` after the second review of #109 (the «erase in progress» entry by its key, the full NVS, `GW44`): **168/168 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM, **169/169**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). The *rollback* of `GW40` had 2 196 and 2 300 B free in `fw_trial`. The earlier run, with the new *fixture* of `GW41` (`gw` on page 0), failed the check of #94 in both variants. After cut 3, the NVS listed the entry under another namespace, and the migration did not copy it or erase anything. The fix skips the entry by its key (842aa16).

Result of `fix/nvs-edge` (issue #109 and point 1 of #107, with `main` 7681914, that is `fix/e2e-harness`, included): **167/167 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM, **168/168**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). The deadline *rollback* of `GW40` left 2 260 and 2 300 B free in `fw_trial`. The harness now measures these values on the real path (approximately 3.9 KB used). The rollback of an image that rejects the configuration left 2 552 B free in the `main` task (6 KB). Before, on the same tree, a run without PSRAM gave 162/166. QEMU died two times in the #97 scenario `otarej` (SIGSEGV, the emulator fault of #107 point 2, with another QEMU run in parallel on the same host). Also, three checks of the main scenario failed, which this branch does not change (mDNS, the *panics* of #65 and of #62). The retry passed completely. On the earlier base (fd2316f), the run without PSRAM gave 159/162 for the same cause (SIGSEGV in `sf spill`, then *store & forward*, IO-7 and the global seq). With PSRAM: 163/163.

Result after the review of #108 (second round of `fix/ota-trial`): **160/160 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`), **161/161**. Both runs had no retries (`retries` empty, `qemu-exits.txt`: `none`). The *rollback* of `GW40` left 2 468 and 2 508 B of free stack in `fw_trial`. First round (issues #99, #101, #102, #103 point 1, #104 points 1 and 2): 157/157 and 158/158, also without retries. Before these two rounds, four runs without PSRAM failed for causes outside the firmware under test. Each cause got a fix or a retry. (1) QEMU was silent in the deadline *rollback* of `GW40`, two times in sequence, and `GW43` had `A stack overflow in task esp_timer`. This is the probable cause of §6.10, now fixed (the strings are now out of the stack of the verification). (2) The harness typed the `panic` of `GW40` before the shell read it (the trial is now announced before the shell, #101). Also, during the wait for the shell, QEMU froze one time while it wrote the *core dump*, with the configuration half loaded. The harness now waits for the `gw_fw_init` line, the earlier point. Also, a `LoadStorePIFAddrError` on SYSTIMER occurred during the verification of an upload in `GW43` (the QEMU fault of §4.3). (3) The «corrupt» image of `GW40` was valid by chance: the old checksum agreed with the version `0.1.0+gcdbd5bb` (XOR 0; `fw_image` now inverts one bit). (4) The check of #98 read the console line before the line arrived (now the check waits for it). Earlier runs: **152/152 checks OK** (on `feature/firmware` with `main` a0b4a10 included, after the fixes of issues #93, #94 (with its second and third review), #98 and of point 5 of #99: FW-1/FW-2, #67, #80, #84, #86, #87, #88, #89, #90, #94 and #98; no QEMU retry in that run, `retries` empty in `results.json`). With the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`, #85): **153/153**, also without retries (see «Octal PSRAM» below). An earlier run, with the same code, gave 150/152. QEMU stopped responding in `esp_ota_mark_app_invalid_rollback_and_reboot()` (it did not end, so the harness does not count it as a QEMU death). This occurred after `Take trusted digest key(s) from running app`, at the return from `e2e-v3` when the deadline expired. The same trace occurred on this branch before these changes (§6.10). After the second review of #94, one run gave 151/152. In `GW42`, the image without NVS encryption had a *panic* on core 1 (`LoadStorePIFAddrError` at `0x6002306c`, SYSTIMER). The *panic* occurred during the verification of the image that the gateway downloaded a second time. This is the same type of QEMU fault as the cache freeze of §4.3; the next run passed completely. with `--panics 3`, on `feature/sparkplug-metrics`, the three dumps decoded (#62).

Result with issues #91, #100 and #103 (branch `fix/web-hardening` on `main` cf6fc7c): **155/155 checks OK** and, with the octal PSRAM (`E2E_PSRAM=1`), **156/156**. In both runs, `retries` is empty in `results.json`, and QEMU did not exit when the harness did not request it. The three new checks are the check of #91 (document print) and the two checks of #100 (CSRF and the web UI).

| Criterion | Measured result |
|---|---|
| CFG-1 / CFG-2: boots with a `config.json` of 131 073 B, an empty one, one with a syntax error, one with a schema error and one with the partition erased; and without a file | OK: in the first five, polling and MQTT do not start (no RS485 UART, 0 NBIRTH, 0 Modbus requests; only the recovery network and the web). The LED changes to error, and the ERROR log gives the reason (`… 131073 bytes; el máximo es 131072`, `línea 5, columna 3`, `devices[0].tags[0].type`, `no montable`). Without a file: the embedded configuration |
| §7.1 NBIRTH with seq = 0 and bdSeq; when the test kills the TCP connection (SIGKILL to QEMU), the broker publishes the NDEATH with the same bdSeq | OK: NBIRTH 1.9 s after boot; NDEATH with the bdSeq of the session |
| Broker restart (SIGTERM, stopped for 9 s) | OK: mosquitto publishes the will (NDEATH, bdSeq of the session) when it closes. Backoff 1000 → 1866 → 3443 → 6860 ms, with a new bdSeq at each attempt. NBIRTH (seq 0, new bdSeq) and DBIRTH of the two devices (seq 1 and 2) |
| Frozen broker (SIGSTOP 20 s > 1.5 × keepalive) | OK: the gateway declares the session dead because of the keepalive after 10.0 s (`No PING_RESP`). After SIGCONT, the wills of the dead session and of the attempt during the freeze arrive. Then the NBIRTH with a new bdSeq and the two DBIRTHs arrive. The new session stays alive |
| §7.2 Slave off → DDEATH; slave on → DBIRTH | OK: DDEATH in 3.47 s (bound 5.8 s); DBIRTH 1.52 s after the return (bound: the probe limit of 10 s plus one cycle) |
| MB-9 probe with backoff of the offline devices (three units that are not on the bus); #61 | OK: unit 3 gets only one attempt per probe **from boot**, without full cycles, at 2.0 / 4.3 / 8.0 / 10.0 s. Meter1 does 21 cycles in its first 19 s, maximum interval 1.68 s |
| MB-6 new types | OK: DBIRTH with `Energy` UInt64 = exact 2^53 + 1, `Counter` Int64 = −(2^53 + 1) (CDAB), `Flow` Double (DCBA), `Temp` Int32 −123 456 (BADC), `Serial` String `SN-0042-ABCD`, `Alarm`/`Ready` Boolean (bits 3 and 15). DCMD `Counter` = 2^62 + 3 → **one** FC16 of 4 registers in CDAB order, and an exact DDATA. DCMD `Alarm` = false → FC03 and FC06 with 0x0101 (the other bits stay). DCMD `Serial` = `HELLO` → FC16 of 8 registers |
| MB-11 / #38 gap per device | OK: Meter1 (`max_gap: 10`) requests exactly coil 0, holding 10–21, 30–33, 34–37, 50–57, 60 and input 0–1, 40–46. Bulk (firmware gap 0) continues with one FC03 of 60 |
| MB-12 slow scan | OK: in 12 s, 12 fast cycles (every 1 s) and 4 reads of `Energy` and `Serial` at 3.04 / 3.00 / 3.01 s |
| MB-14 write between blocks | OK: with responses of 120 ms, the test sends a DCMD in the middle of a Meter1 cycle. The gateway writes it between the requests of that cycle (1 before, 4 after in the same cycle). Latency 0.02–0.15 s from the publish |
| SPB-15 Scan Rate | OK: NBIRTH `Node Control/Scan Rate` UInt32 = 0; NCMD 3000 → NDATA 3000 and Meter1 every 3.0 s; NCMD 10 → discarded with a log, NDATA 3000; NCMD 0 → NDATA 0 and again every 1.0 s |
| MB-15 Modbus TCP | OK: a pymodbus client on port 502 reads holding 10 of unit 1 (= 123, the Setpoint), and writes holding 200 = 4242 (0.11 s). Dead unit 3 → exception 0B. FC 0x2B → 01 from the gateway, not sent to the bus. In the separate network boot (`allowed_clients: ["192.0.2.0/24"]`), the gateway closes the connection from 10.0.2.2 without a response, and records it in the log |
| #69 Modbus TCP counters | OK: after that run, `devices` gives `Meter1 … \| tcp ok 2 timeout 0 …`, `Dead3 … \| tcp ok 0 timeout 3 …` and `modbus_tcp connections 2 accepted 0 rejected \| forwarded 3 \| gateway exceptions 01 1 03 0 0A 0 0B 1 \| other units …`. In the boot with the restricted list: `connections 0 accepted 1 rejected` |
| #68 `config` summary | OK: `Meter1 unit 1, poll 1000 ms, slow 3000 ms, offline after 3, 11 tag(s) (9 fast, 2 slow), blocks max_gap 10`; `Bulk … blocks max_gap 0 (default)`; `modbus_tcp enabled, port 502, idle timeout 60 s, clients 10.0.2.2/32` |
| #65 *panic* with a Modbus response in transit | OK: unit 1 responds 120 ms late. Three *panics* occur immediately after one of its requests. All three boots come back with NBIRTH and `Reset Reason` = `panic` |
| MQTT-2: broker that cuts the connection after the CONNACK; rejected SUBACK (0x80) | OK: the backoff continues to increase (1000 → 1696 → 3492 → 7681 ms). With the rejected SUBACK there is no NBIRTH. The gateway sends DISCONNECT and tries again (1000 → 1861 → 3703 ms). Since #89, the check waits for the end of each of the three rejected connections, with a limit of 15 s. It requires a DISCONNECT in each connection and a backoff that increases strictly. The backoff goes back to the minimum 10.1 s (one keepalive) after the NBIRTH of a stable session |
| REL-1: watchdog and *core dump* | OK: the births go out in 9 ms, with a maximum of 4 ms without a TWDT feed. `panic` in the shell → "Core dump has been saved to flash", NBIRTH with `Reset Reason` = `panic`, and `esp_coredump` decodes the dump (trace through `panic_task` and `abort`) |
| Core affinity | OK: `tasks rs485_bus 1 sparkplug 0 tcpip 0 mqtt_task 0 emac_rx 0 sys_evt 0` |
| §7.3 DI → NDATA in less than 100 ms; NCMD DO/3 = true → NDATA | OK: 23 ms (includes 20 ms of debounce); NDATA alias 22 = true (simulated pins with the polarity of `gw_bsp`) |
| IO-3 safe state (`failsafe_s` 5, DO/2 `on`, DO/4 `hold`, the other DOs `off`) | OK: the NBIRTH of the first boot has only DO/2 on. Broker down for 9 s → `failsafe` after 5.1 s (DO on 0x02, off 0xc5: the rule DOs stay out). The next NBIRTH has DO/2 on, DO/3 off (an NCMD had set it on) and DO/4 kept. After a cut (a new QEMU), DO/4 boots off. After NCMD Reboot, DO/4 stays on (`held across the restart 0x08 of 0x08`; the expander model keeps its registers in RTC memory) |
| IO-4 pulse counters | OK: NBIRTH with `Counter/1`…`8` UInt64 at 0. `di 1 1` → one NDATA with DI/1 and `Counter/1` = 1. The periodic save (60 s) stays after the cut (`Counter/1` = 1 after it). Another activation and NCMD Reboot → a save before the restart, and `Counter/1` = 2 after it |
| IO-6 buzzer (patterns in the log of the QEMU build) | OK: the `device offline` alarm 5 s after boot (Dead3–5). `MQTT down` 5.4 s after the loss of the session, and `device offline` again 0.2 s after the NBIRTH. A boot with a broken `config.json` on the same flash → `configuration error` (3 beeps), from the data in NVS. Button: 1 beep at 3 s, 2 beeps at the factory reset |
| IO-7 local rules (`follow` DI/2 → DO/5, `toggle` DI/3 → DO/6) | OK: `di 2 1` → NDATA with DO/5 on in 27 ms (debounce included). NCMD DO/5 = false is rejected (`driven by local rule 0`), and NDATA gives the real state. Two activations of DI/3 → DO/6 on, then off. With the broker down, one activation sets DO/6 on, and the next NBIRTH shows it |
| §7.4 DCMD Setpoint = 12.3 → holding 10 = 123 by FC06, and DDATA | OK: the slave recorded `(6, 10, [123])`; DDATA 12.3. Float32 `Limit` = 1234.5: **only one** FC16 of 2 registers, and DDATA 1234.5 |
| §7.5 NCMD Rebirth → NBIRTH (seq 0, same bdSeq) and all DBIRTHs | OK |
| §7.6 All payloads decode with the official proto | OK: 2065 messages, 0 errors |
| CFG-6 shell on the console; REL-5 | OK: `devices` gives `Meter1 unit 1 online cycles 2 failed 0 \| ok 6 …` and, with the slave off, `offline … failed 3 \| … timeout 9`. `config` gives the summary. `status` responds with the valid configuration (`config … ok`, `MQTT connected`, `Modbus 2/5 devices online`) and also with the rejected configuration. NBIRTH `Node Info/Reset Reason` = `power-on` at the first boot, and `software` after NCMD Reboot |
| NET-4 without radio and NET-7: configuration with `network.wifi` and `network.ipv6` (node `GW02`, separate boot) in the QEMU build | OK: the gateway boots, gives the warning `network.wifi ignored: Wi-Fi is not available in this build` and publishes NBIRTH through Ethernet. The shell shows `iface ethernet up 10.0.2.15 (active) \| wifi not configured` and the summary of `network.wifi`. With the normal configuration, `status` shows Ethernet as the only active interface. IPv6: `fe80::5054:ff:fe12:3456` (link-local), `fec0::5054:ff:fe12:3456` (SLAAC with the RA of slirp) and `fec0::1234` (static); `ping fec0::2`: 3 sent, 3 answered. It is not possible to test MQTT over IPv6 here (§6.6) |
| REL-4 remote syslog (UDP to the receiver of the harness) | OK: `<134>1 2026-11-04T01:03:09.035Z GW01 edge - - [meta sequenceId="21"] Sparkplug session up (bdSeq=0)`: *facility* local0, HOSTNAME = node, APP-NAME = module, SNTP time. 22 datagrams at the first NBIRTH, 0 malformed. `status` gives `syslog 10.0.2.2:15514 connected, sent 22, dropped 0, errors 0`. With the broker down, `<132> … MQTT down; reconnect attempt 1 in 1000 ms` arrives. 119 datagrams in 3 boots, `sequenceId` without gaps in each boot |
| CFG-3 / CFG-4 recovery (five rejected files) | OK: the network starts (DHCP). The web and `/api/status` respond with the commissioning password (read from the console) and the reason. 0 Modbus requests, 0 NBIRTH. With the schema error, the test exports the document, corrects it and saves it through the API. The gateway restarts and publishes NBIRTH (`GW03`) |
| CFG-4 REST API (node `GW04`) | OK: without credentials or with a different password → 401; `GET` with `mqtt.password` and `web.password` = `********`; invalid `PUT` → 400 `devices[0].tags[0].type`; syntax → 400 with line 5, column 9; `dry_run` → 200 without a save; 131 074 B → 413; valid `PUT` → NDEATH of `GW04`, NBIRTH of `GW05`. The same password stays valid |
| CFG-3 web UI | OK: `/` (≈14 KB, HTML) and `/api/status` (mode, MQTT, `Meter1` online, `Voltage` = 230.5, Ethernet active) |
| CFG-6 configuration shell | OK: `config show` with 2 masked secrets and no plaintext secret; `mbread 1 holding 10` = 50, `mbread 1 input 0 2` = 17254 32768 (230.5), unit 9 → error; `rebirth` → NBIRTH seq 0 and DBIRTH seq 1 and 2; invalid `config load` → path; valid `config check` without a save; valid `config load` → NDEATH, NBIRTH of `GW06` |
| CFG-7 secrets | OK: at the first boot, `secrets moved from /cfg/config.json to NVS`; `NVS partition "nvs" is encrypted` (QEMU emulates HMAC and eFuse). The shell shows `NVS encrypted (HMAC key in eFuse KEY5)`. The MQTT password and the web password do not show as plaintext at any location in the flash image |
| CFG-5 factory reset and NET-5 | OK: `boot 1` for 3 s → access point gesture (no reset; the QEMU build gives a warning that there is no radio). `boot 1` held → warning at 2.1 s, reset at 10.1 s, an orderly NDEATH, boot in commissioning (blue LED, 0 NBIRTH, not even the example identity). The old web password → 401. The new commissioning password opens `/api/status` (`commissioning`). A configuration loaded through the API boots the gateway (`GW08`). No block of the old configuration stays in the partition |
| NET-6 | OK: `mdns: gw01.local, _https._tcp port 443 ('Plant1/GW01')` (name derived from `edge_node_id`; with HTTPS, the gateway announces the HTTPS port). It is not possible to test the responses with `open_eth` (§6.2.1) |
| MQTT-4 TLS (nodes `GW12`, `GW14`, `GW15`) | OK: a TLS 1.3-only listener with an own CA: NBIRTH 3.7 s after boot, `TLS: ca_file: 1 certificate(s) from /cfg/ca.pem`, name verified against the SAN `IP:10.0.2.2`. A `server_name` that the certificate does not carry → `the broker certificate does not carry the expected name` (mbedTLS -0x2700, *flags* 0x4). A second line gives the CN, the SANs, the validity, the expected name and the clock (issue #70); no session. Expired certificate (`GW25`, TLS 1.3) → `the broker certificate expired` (*flags* 0x1), with its validity, the clock and `SNTP synchronised`. Missing CA → `mqtt.tls.ca_file: /cfg/missing.pem: no existe…: MQTT not started`, no attempts. Free stack after the NBIRTH: sparkplug 3628 of 6144 B, `mqtt_task` 2360 B |
| MQTT-5 mutual TLS (`GW13`, `GW16`) | OK: The listener accepts only TLS 1.2, with `require_certificate`. NBIRTH. Mosquitto records `as GW13 (p2, c1, k10, u'GW13')` (the CN of the certificate is the user name). The gateway verifies the name against `tls.server_name` (`DNS:broker.e2e`). Without a client certificate, the broker cuts the handshake |
| MQTT-6 broker list (`GW17`) | OK: Primary broker stopped → 2 connection attempts and NBIRTH on the backup broker. Primary broker available again → NDEATH on the backup broker and NBIRTH on the primary broker 20.1 s later (keepalive of 10 s + `primary_return_s` 10 s). Primary broker stopped again → 2 connection attempts and NBIRTH on the backup broker at 3.8 s. The shell shows the current broker and the list |
| MQTT-8 MQTT 5 (`GW18`) | OK: Mosquitto records `(p5, c1, k10)`. NBIRTH. NCMD Rebirth arrives through the v5 subscription. When the connection stops, the NDEATH of the *will* comes immediately, with the bdSeq of the session |
| SPB-13 primary host (`GW19`) | OK: Retained STATE `false` → the gateway subscribes and does not publish NBIRTH (the shell: `host primary 'SCADA1' offline, NBIRTH waits for it`). `true` → NBIRTH at 0.03 s. `false` with an old timestamp → no effect. `false` → NDEATH with the bdSeq of the session, a disconnection, and a wait in the next session. `true` → NBIRTH with a new bdSeq |
| SPB-14 store & forward (`GW20`) | OK: The relay removes the broker. During that time, Voltage is 240/241/242, and DI/3 changes to on and then to off. When the broker is available again: NBIRTH, DBIRTH with 242, then historical DDATA/NDATA (`is_historical`) with 240, 241, 242 and DI/3 `true`, `false`, in timestamp sequence. The ring holds 512 samples in internal RAM (QEMU without PSRAM). The shell counts the pending samples and the sent samples |
| Issue #59 slow link (`GW21`) | OK: The relay operates at 700 B/s. *Rebirths* fill the buffers. Then the log shows `publish of 2685 bytes to …/DBIRTH/GW21/Bulk still running after 2000 ms`, and the publish operation returns at 2022 ms. The connection closes, with no *panic*. With the fast link, a new NBIRTH |
| Issue #64 HTTPS (`GW22`) | OK: The gateway generates the certificate at the first boot. The SHA-256 fingerprint on the console is equal to the fingerprint of the certificate that the server shows. HTTP → 301 (GET) and 308 (PUT) to `https://`. 5 failures → 429 with `Retry-After` 29 s, also with the correct password. Log `10.0.2.2 blocked for 30 s after 5 failed logins in a row` and counters in the shell. After the block time, 200. The other web scenarios (recovery mode, API, commissioning) use HTTPS |
| Issue #100 CSRF (`GW22`, before the login limit) | OK: The test uses the correct credentials (a browser keeps them in its cache). `PUT /api/config?dry_run=1` → 403 in these conditions: no `X-Requested-With`, an `Origin` from a different site, `Origin: null`, a `Referer` from a different site, or an `http://` `Origin`. `POST /api/restart` without the header → 403 (no restart). `POST /api/firmware` `text/plain` without the header (the *no-cors* `fetch` of the issue) → 403. The same request with a URL from a different origin → 403. An `Origin` from a different site with the **incorrect** password → 403, not 401 (the origin check occurs before the password check). The same origin with `text/plain` → 415. Log `… refused: cross-origin request`, shell `logins failed 0 … cross-site writes refused 9`. The request form of the web UI (the header, its own `Origin` and `Referer`) → 200. Only `Referer` → 200. A script with the header and without `Origin` → 200. The page sends the header in its two types of write request |
| Issue #106 bodies of refused requests (`GW22`) | OK: Upload of 1.5 MB at 150 KB/s. HTTPS without credentials → 401, and from a different site → 403. `GET /api/status` half a second later (on a session that opened before) takes 0.37 / 0.31 s. The gateway cuts the upload at 49 152 B. HTTP port → 308, `GET` in 0.02 s, and the gateway cuts the upload at 32 768 B. Shell `bodies cut 9`. Reference run with the cut disabled: `GET` on the HTTP port in 11.51 s, and the three uploads are complete. The 413 of an authenticated 131 KB `PUT` still arrives (web scenario). An authenticated, same-origin 1.5 MB upload that is not an image: the gateway discards all of it. The client sends all of it, as a browser does, and then reads the 4xx with its reason |
| Issues #31, #122 and #124 spill to the card (`GW23`) | OK. **Without PSRAM** (the default variant): the spill does not start. The boot log and the shell show `spill    off: it needs PSRAM, and none was detected`, and the card is not used. **With PSRAM** (a ring of 1 000 samples in this build, a LittleFS directory in place of the card with a "cluster" of 32 B: 32 extents of 4 KB for `max_kb` 128): (1) The image holds 3 old files and a file `80000001.sf` that is not ours. After the network starts, the boot recovery takes 59 ms (2 reads) and makes a new ring. The loop deletes the old files in exactly 11 steps (mark, truncates of 4 KB, unlink). (2) An outage of 40 s with all 60 `Bulk` registers changed twice a second: 2 400 samples wait, 1 690 of them in 10 blocks on the card. After the reconnection, all 2 400 arrive as historical DDATA, in sequence, none overwritten or lost. A checkpoint follows (at most 1 690 samples replayed between two). (3) A second outage, then a power cut (QEMU killed) with 845 samples in 5 blocks on the card. After the reboot, the recovery finds them (1 095 ms, 34 reads), and the replay sends them one time, in sequence, with no value of the first outage. (4) A new image with two segments of the earlier format of this configuration (150 samples): they arrive one time as historical DDATA, then the loop deletes them in 4 steps. `card format` asks for `confirm`; `card format confirm` refuses (no card in QEMU). The `card` line shows the times in QEMU (`block` max 31.0 ms, `super` max 17.5 ms, `alloc` max 323.6 ms, 0 errors). The longest time without a watchdog feed of `sparkplug` was 1 003 ms, and its stack kept 2 756 B free |

The harness changes the 60 Bulk registers until the RAM ring holds 300 to 749 samples (below three quarters, so the spill moved 0 samples). Then the shell `restart`: the log shows `before the restart, 360 samples (360 records) moved from the RAM ring to the card in 794 ms (3 blocks, 7 card operations)`. After the boot, `card ready …: 360 samples waiting`. When the broker is back, the historical DDATA has exactly 360 metrics: the values of this outage only, one time, in order. The same with `POST /api/restart` (300 samples, 835 ms). The `sparkplug` stack kept 2 788 B free |

The table does not have a row for each check. These checks are in the result paragraphs above, in §6.2.1, §6.9 and §6.10, and in the check names of `results.json`:

- #97: a new image that rejects the configuration goes back at once, an image in commissioning is confirmed, and the NVS migration starts on a device without a burned KEY5;
- #109: the full unencrypted NVS (`GW44`), a cut before the erase with LittleFS out of reach, and the way out for the operator;
- #110: the *slowloris* client, `--web-stress`, and the stack thresholds (`httpd` ≥ 2048 B, `httpd_redirect` and `web_guard` ≥ 512 B);
- #111: the park of core 1 during a console flood, and the final check that no park got to its bound or was refused;
- #112: `GW47`, a park during an RS485 transaction of more than 30 s;
- #87: a firmware URL longer than the limits is refused with its reason;
- IO-2: the DOs go off before the configuration loads.

**What QEMU does not cover**, and why the emulator build is different (`sdkconfig.qemu`):

- **W5500.** QEMU does not emulate it. The build uses `open_eth`.
- **TCA9554, GPIO and RMT.** QEMU does not emulate them. The build uses `CONFIG_GW_IO_SIMULATED`: the DOs are in RAM, the DIs come from the `di` shell command, and the LED is only in the log.
- **RS485 mode of the UART.** `uart_wait_tx_done` causes an `assert` on `TX_DONE`. The build uses the normal UART mode.
- **Low baud rates.** The E2E operates at 9600 baud. Only the host test covers the wait for the end of transmission at 1200 baud.
- **Task watchdog.** QEMU runs with `wdt_disable` on the timers, thus the TWDT cannot trigger. The E2E uses the log to check that the Sparkplug task feeds the TWDT between publish operations. It does not use a real slow link.
- **TCA9554 absent.** The simulated expander always answers, unless the shell tells it not to answer (`expander absent <ms>`: silent for the first ms of the next boot). The time starts at its first access, because in QEMU `esp_timer` does not return to 0 after a software restart (`docs/upstream-reports.md` §5; #74). Thus the E2E tests the retry in commissioning mode. Only host tests cover a real I²C failure. The registers of the expander are in RTC memory that the boot does not erase. Thus, as on the chip, the registers keep their values after a software restart or a *panic*. They return to their power-on values only at a cold boot (new QEMU).
- **Buzzer.** There is no LEDC and no GPIO46: the QEMU build only writes the beeps and the alarms to the log.
- ***Core dump* and the other core (#62).** **Cause:** the *panic handler* tells the other core to stop (`esp_cpu_stall`, the *software stall* of `RTC_CNTL`), and QEMU does not emulate this. The other core continues to run tasks while the *panic handler* writes the dump. The log shows this: the `buzzer` (a different task) continued to write during the erase of the `coredump` partition. In one run, the ELF writer aborted inside the *panic* with "Re-entered core dump" (`ESP_COREDUMP_ASSERT` in `core_dump_flash.c:158`: the task data grew between the pass that calculates the size and the pass that writes). In other runs, the checksum was not written (`a5a5…`), or the dump was truncated (`stream read less than specified amount`). **Deterministic check:** the `panic` command of the QEMU shell first parks the other core, as the *stall* of the chip does. To do this, it runs a function that loops with the interrupts disabled, through `esp_ipc_call`, from a task pinned to core 0. Then it aborts: one *panic* only, and its dump must decode (`--panics N` repeats it N times for a measurement). The 1 s delay before the restart stays. On the board, the other core really stops: a real dump must still confirm this (the campaign of #18). The upstream report of the cause is espressif/qemu#189, with PR #190. The report of the dump symptom, with a minimal reproduction, is in `docs/upstream-reports.md` §3 (#82).
- **Check of the refused SUBACK with a loaded host (#89).** One run under load saw 3 refusals and 2 DISCONNECT packets. **Cause: the harness.** The firmware does not leave a connection open. esp-mqtt writes the DISCONNECT (`send_disconnect_msg`), closes the connection, and only then sends the disconnect event. Only after that event does the gateway record `reconnect attempt N`. But the DISCONNECT goes through slirp and through the thread of the fake broker of the harness, and the log line goes through the console. The check counted the DISCONNECT packets as soon as the line `reconnect attempt 3` appeared, and then killed QEMU immediately. Thus, with a loaded host, the line could arrive before the packet. **Now** the fake broker records three items for each connection: if the broker refused its SUBSCRIBE, if the DISCONNECT arrived, and if the connection ended. The check waits, for up to 15 s, until the first three refused connections end. It requires a DISCONNECT on each of them. A connection that closes without one, or that does not end in time, fails, and the report shows it as such, together with the esp-mqtt error if there is one. The check also requires that the backoff increases at each attempt (before, it required only `delays[2] > delays[0]`). The details also show how many DISCONNECT packets there were when the line appeared. Under the same load (`tools/e2e/cpu_load.py 2`), the problem did not occur again: 12 of 12 with `--only refuse` (the check alone, a new scenario) and 2 of 2 in `main`. In all of them, the 3 DISCONNECT packets were already counted when the line arrived. Thus the race is rare.
- **Invalid *core dumps* with a loaded host (#88).** The 2 of 9 runs of `feature/firmware` with the three invalid dumps **did not have #62**. The git history of that branch shows this: the branch merged `main` with #62 (6bec093) at 09:11, after those runs (07:11 to 09:05, commits up to 1e23b2e). Its check was still the old check (`cmd_panic`/`abort`, the check that did retries). The test reproduced the problem with a bounded load (`tools/e2e/cpu_load.py 2 1800 &`: two busy loop processes, on a host with 4 CPUs) and `run_e2e.sh --only main --panics N`:
  - with #62: **21 of 21 valid dumps** (runs of 6, 10 and 5, the last one with the final check). With only the park operation removed, and with the `panic` task pinned to core 0, 6 of 6 more;
  - with the old `panic` (`abort()` in the console task, nothing parked): 1 of 6 invalid. The writer recorded the full dump (`check sum length 32`, `saved`), but the checksum did not get to the flash. The gateway itself read it as the erased pattern (`Core dump data check failed`; `esp_coredump`: `should be "a5a5…"`). Between `Save core dump to flash` and the restart, the `buzzer` task on the other core continued to write to the log.

  **Conclusion:** the cause is the emulator (the missing *stall*, espressif/qemu#189; added to `docs/upstream-reports.md` §3), not the firmware path. **The check now**:
  - accepts a dump as valid only if it decodes, and if the boot check of the gateway (`CONFIG_ESP_COREDUMP_CHECK_BOOT`: `Core dump data checksum is correct`) accepts it;
  - reports an invalid dump as an emulator fault if the log shows a different task in the dump window, or a nested `abort` (`Re-entered core dump`). Then it repeats the *panic*, up to two times for each run. (A test of the classification on the saved logs marks the invalid dump above and none of the valid dumps.);
  - fails immediately for any other invalid dump (a failure of the firmware writer, without that signature).

  For each attempt, the harness keeps the UART log (`uart0-panic-N.log`), the dump (`coredump-N.bin`) and `coredump-info-N.txt`.
- **Octal PSRAM (#63, #85).** The E2E also runs with the octal PSRAM. `E2E_PSRAM=1 tools/e2e/run_e2e.sh` compiles `build-qemu-psram` (`sdkconfig.qemu-psram`). QEMU emulates the PSRAM with `-global driver=ssi_psram,property=is_octal,value=true` and `-m 8M` (the size on the board). With `-m 32M`, QEMU maps 31 MB, and no virtual space remains for the *mmap* of the flash. **Result: 153/153** on `feature/firmware`. This includes the usual 152 checks, with the *store & forward* on the ring of 40 000 samples in PSRAM instead of the internal ring of 512. The FW-1 and #67 checks also run in this variant. One more check is the PSRAM location of the configuration (32 304 B), of the Sparkplug engine (21 480 B) and of its payload buffer (16 384 B). This variant is in CI: the `psram` jobs of `e2e`, which run the full run in 5 parallel groups (2241 s of scenarios in total, §4.5). There are two QEMU defects, each with a workaround only in the emulator:
  - **SIGSEGV on the first write of `otadata`** by the bootloader on a new image (`Set actual ota_seq=1 in otadata[0]`). The SPI1 transaction gets to the PSRAM model, which reads its buffer at the address -1 (`psram_quad_read`, `hw/misc/ssi_psram.c:154`; report in `docs/upstream-reports.md` §4). Before the boot, the harness writes a valid `otadata` entry into the image. This entry is identical to the entry that the bootloader writes (`ota_seq` 1, valid state, CRC32). Thus no boot writes it (`write_otadata` in `e2e.py`). With this workaround, there was no crash in two full runs and two partial runs.
  - **The GDMA does not get to the PSRAM** ([espressif/qemu#180](https://github.com/espressif/qemu/issues/180), open). With PSRAM, mbedTLS allocates its buffers there, and the SHA and AES accelerators in DMA mode receive no data. Thus the first TLS *handshake* (HTTPS or MQTT) did not complete (the CFG-3 recovery mode tests failed with a *timeout*). `sdkconfig.qemu-psram` uses software SHA and AES (`CONFIG_MBEDTLS_HARDWARE_SHA=n`, `CONFIG_MBEDTLS_HARDWARE_AES=n`). The board keeps the accelerators, and only the hardware can verify that path (DMA from PSRAM).
  
  QEMU does not validate these items of the real PSRAM: the timing, the chip detection, and the *cache* with data in transit.
- **QIO flash.** The reads of the application arrive with an offset of 2 bytes. The build uses DIO.
