- **IPv6 to the host.** slirp sends the connections to `fec0::2` to the `::1` of the host, and the kernel of the container has no IPv6. Thus the E2E tests only the addresses and ICMPv6.
- **UART1 after a restart.** IDF resets UART0 at exit, but not UART1. The chip clears UART1 in `uart_driver_install()`, but the QEMU model does not. Sometimes a slave sent a response at the time of the restart. Then the interrupt line stayed active permanently, and the next boot stopped at the driver installation (seen with gdb on the QEMU *gdbstub*; 2 of 3 E2E runs after NCMD Reboot or a *panic*). `gw_rs485` masks the interrupts of the port before each restart. It uses a *shutdown handler* for `esp_restart()` and a wrapper of `esp_restart_noos()` (`-Wl,--wrap`) that also covers the *panic*. 40 restarts in sequence (20 NCMD Reboot, 20 *panic*) gave no hang. The E2E checks this with three *panic* events immediately when a Modbus response is in transit (#65). A clear operation of the UART1 interrupts at boot, before `uart_driver_install()`, is not sufficient in QEMU (tested: the line stays active with `INT_ENA` = 0). On the board, the driver already resets the UART at installation. On the board, the fix is not necessary, but it does no harm.
- **Wi-Fi.** QEMU does not emulate the radio. The emulator build has `CONFIG_GW_WIFI=n` (and thus `CONFIG_GW_SOFTAP=n`). The E2E checks only two items. A configuration with `network.wifi` boots, gives a warning that there is no Wi-Fi, and continues on Ethernet. The access point triggers get to `gw_ap`.
- **mDNS.** `open_eth` has no multicast filter: the responder starts, but it does not serve the interface.
- **BOOT button.** There is no GPIO: use `boot 1` / `boot 0` in the shell.
- **TLS and store & forward with PSRAM.** In the default QEMU build, without PSRAM, mbedTLS and the store & forward ring use internal RAM (the ring of 512 samples). The `E2E_PSRAM=1` variant runs the ring of 40 000 samples and the TLS sessions in PSRAM (see «Octal PSRAM» above). Only the DMA from PSRAM for the SHA and AES accelerators needs the hardware.
- **Interface switch by broker reachability (issue #57).** Without Wi-Fi, there is no other interface: host tests only.
- **Budget of one publish operation (issue #59).** The E2E runs it with 2 s (`sdkconfig.qemu`). It does not run the 8 s of the board build or the TWDT trigger.
- ***Cache freeze* of the S3 (FW-1).** The firmware verifies an OTA image through a map of its segments. On the S3, a map operation freezes the caches, and it does not stop the other core. QEMU does not model the freeze. If the other core executes code from the flash at that time, QEMU aborts (`Bad ram pointer`). A task on core 1 that calls `snprintf` during `esp_ota_end()` reproduced this in 1 to 3 rounds. The QEMU build keeps the other core in `waiti` during the verification (`CONFIG_GW_FW_PARK_OTHER_CORE`). It does this also during `esp_ota_begin()`, which reads `otadata` through a map (the *rollback* state of the running image). Without this, at the start of a download, core 1 failed one time on a write to a peripheral (`LoadStorePIFAddrError` in `esp_crosscore_int_send`, from the syslog task; a firmware *panic*, not a QEMU death). The QEMU build also does not have the secondary USB-Serial-JTAG console. The connection monitor of that console reads the peripheral at each *tick*, and it also failed during the freeze (`LoadStorePIFAddrError`). The report is in `docs/upstream-reports.md` §6. On the board, this is not necessary. The parked core is always core 1. The *tick* of core 0 also runs the *hook* of the interrupt watchdog, which writes its TIMG and failed in the same way (`LoadStorePIFAddrError` in `reconfigure_ticks`, with an upload that `httpd` served on core 1). For this reason, the QEMU build installs the uploaded image from a task pinned to core 0. **The parked core 1 holds no locks (issue #111, §6.10):** the bus task first stops between two transactions. The firmware writes `fw: parking core 1 (bus task stopped)` before and `fw: core 1 resumed after N ms` after. But QEMU still dies sometimes during an OTA. It shows `iotlb_to_section: Assertion ... failed` during the download, and mostly a SIGSEGV with no message (with the PC in `code_gen_buffer`, the code that TCG generates: 14 of 14 saved cores in #112). One time, it also showed `atomic_mmu_lookup: code should not be reached` (SIGABRT, issue #112, `docs/upstream-reports.md` §8). This did not occur again in 16 runs.

  **Bounded retry (issues #86, #92 and #96).** The harness makes a distinction between emulator faults and firmware faults. A thread monitors each QEMU of the current scenario every half second. **Only two faults can cause a retry:**
  1. QEMU stopped by itself: a signal, an exit code, or a console that closed while the harness used it (#86/#90).
  2. A `LoadStorePIFAddrError` *panic* with `EXCVADDR` in the peripheral space (0x60000000–0x600FFFFF), during a write or a verification of an app image. The evidence for the write or verification is a line from `esp_https_ota`, `esp_image`, `secure_boot_v2`, from the download or from the upload, in the 40 previous lines. Only the lines after the first `app_main` line count (the bootloader also prints `esp_image`).

  A PIF with the PC in IRAM and without that line is a firmware fault (the ISR of the RS485 UART and `mask_port()` are also in IRAM and write to peripherals). The rule for the FreeRTOS *tick* at the boot of a trial image is removed. It occurred only with *heap poisoning*, and its window no longer agrees with the firmware, which reads the other slot in `gw_fw_early_init()`. **A silence never causes a retry** (also not the silence after `Take trusted digest key(s)`): the only case with a dump was a firmware fault (§6.10). The harness detects the silence, dumps the state and kills QEMU immediately. The analysis of the dump stays as diagnostic data in `results.json` (`faults[].analysis`). After the fault, each wait of the harness raises `AttemptAborted`, and the scenario ends there: the harness asserts no check on cut evidence. The harness repeats the full scenario only one time, for a maximum of two scenarios in each run (`MAX_RETRIES`). It does this only if all these conditions are true:
  - the first fault is one of the two faults above;
  - **no check of the attempt failed**;
  - the console showed no firmware fault. These lines are firmware faults:
    - a `Guru Meditation Error` line with any reason (also `Interrupt wdt timeout on CPU`, `Unhandled debug exception`, `Double exception`, `Cache error`), if it is not the known signature;
    - `assert failed:`, `task_wdt: Aborting` or `Task watchdog got triggered`, `ESP_ERROR_CHECK failed`, `CORRUPT HEAP`, `stack overflow`;
    - in the QEMU build, `fw: core 1 park bound reached`, `fw: core 1 park refused` (#111) and `fw: blocking IPC to core 1 while it was parked` (#112);
    - an `abort() was called` or a `Backtrace:`, if it is not the `panic` that the harness requested (its marker `panic requested from the console` in the 20 previous lines of the same emulator) and not the backtrace of the known signature (#96).

  The summary shows these faults anyway (`[PANIC]`). The checks of the discarded attempt, all passed, stay in `results.json` (`retries`: scenario, signature, the fault with its dump, and its decision). **What a retry can hide and what it cannot hide:** a retry does not hide a failed check or a hang. It also does not hide a firmware *panic*, *assert*, watchdog, stack overflow or *abort* that got to the console. A retry can hide what the attempt did not check yet. Sometimes the firmware operates incorrectly immediately before QEMU dies (or before the verification fault), without a print and before the check that can see it. Then the harness discards that attempt, and only the second attempt counts. A regression that kills QEMU systematically still shows, because the second attempt also fails. Any other case, an expired time limit, or a second fault is a failed scenario, with all its checks and the reason for no retry.

  **At any boot (issue #90).** All scenarios use this retry, not only OTA and the NVS migration: QEMU can stop at any boot of any scenario. The harness detects this in three ways:
  - the process stopped because of a signal;
  - the process stopped with an exit code while the harness used it;
  - the emulator console closed (`BrokenPipeError` on a write to it, which the harness changes into `QemuGone`).

  The report says `QEMU terminó (signal N` or `exit status N`, the UART log of that boot`); stderr:` and the last three lines that QEMU wrote in that boot (`uart0-<arranque>.log.qemu`, without the lines of previous boots). At the end, `qemu-exits.txt` lists each QEMU that stopped without a request from the harness. A QEMU that stops before the harness connects its console (#96) gets the same treatment. `kill()` accepts that there is no console, `console()` gives `QemuGone`, and the harness repeats the scenario. Before, `sock` stayed `None`, and the full run aborted. The harness deletes nothing. If a run fails, or if a QEMU stopped by itself, the harness says so. The work directory, each `uart0-*.log` and each `uart0-*.log.qemu` stay (also the logs of a discarded first attempt: the harness opens the logs in *append* mode). CI also uploads `qemu-exits.txt` and the `fault-*.txt` dumps.

  **What an attempt leaves open (issue #105).** A scenario that ends because of an exception does not do its own cleanup. Before, the MQTT observer of that attempt stayed subscribed to `spBv1.0/#`, and each subsequent scenario saw each message two times (CI of `main` cf6fc7c, `e2e-psram`: 146/153 after a retry of `failsafe`; `DBIRTH seq [1, 1, 2, 2]`, SPB-14, IO-7 and SPB-5). Now the harness registers the observers, the *relays*, the fake brokers and the OTA image servers with the attempt that created them. At the end of each attempt, `run_scenario` closes the items that are still open and kills the brokers that the attempt started. It also writes a message about this (`[CLEANUP]`). An observer of an attempt that ended adds nothing to `CAPTURE`, and a final check requires that no such message arrived. The self-test `--simulate-qemu-death ota` also leaves an observer subscribed to the main broker. With `--only ota,slow`, the `slow` scenario after the retry sees no duplicates, and SPB-5 passes. The fake broker releases its port when it closes (`shutdown()` wakes its `accept()`, and the harness waits for the thread). Thus the harness can repeat a scenario that uses the fake broker. The harness kills the brokers that the attempt started. This includes the replacement of the main broker that `main` starts in its MQTT-2 check (`mosquitto2`). Before each attempt, if the main broker does not run, the harness starts a new one (`mosquitto-restarted-N.log`), and `main` receives it. Also, a retry starts from the same device as the first attempt. The harness saves the image under test and the eFuse file at the start of each scenario, and restores them before the retry. After each attempt that ended with a fault or an exception, with or without a retry, the harness returns the Modbus slave to its state at the start of that attempt. This state includes the registers and bits of the two units, the refused addresses and the response delay. The reason is that `run_store_forward` and the *spill* scenario restore the slave only at the end. (`main` boots the image under test itself: without this restore, its second attempt saw the counters, the bdSeq and the DCMD values of the first attempt.) Self-test: `--simulate-qemu-death main` kills the emulator after that broker replacement. The retry finds a broker and passes completely (76/76 with `--only main`).

  **Hung QEMU and time limit for each scenario (issue #92).** A live QEMU with no UART output for `--hang-timeout` seconds (60 by default) counts as hung, if this occurs in a segment where the firmware always prints. These segments are a boot (from `ESP-ROM:` to the first `app_main` line) and the signature check of an image. After `Take trusted digest key(s)`, the limit is 30 s, because the next line arrives in milliseconds. Before the harness kills QEMU, it saves these items to `fault-NN-<log>.txt`:
  - two `info registers -a` outputs from the QEMU monitor (a UNIX socket for each emulator), with one second between them;
  - the PC of each core, with its region (ROM, IRAM, flash);
  - the last 40 lines of the console;
  - the stderr of QEMU.

  `results.json` → `faults` gives a summary of each fault (scenario, attempt, type, signature, time, PC, and if the monitor answered). No silence causes a retry: the scenario fails, with the dump and its analysis in `faults[].analysis` (PCs that stay or change, cores in a wait state). The harness takes the dump with the emulator reserved: if the scenario calls `kill()` during that time, the call waits. `run_scenario` takes the same lock as the monitor before it reads the faults and before it opens the next attempt. Each fault has the key of its attempt. Thus a fault (or its dump, which can take up to 7 s) never goes into the next scenario. If the monitor itself raises an exception, the monitor records it and continues to monitor, and the run fails (a final check). Also, each scenario attempt has a time limit: 900 s by default. This is less than two times the longest measured scenario (the #97 integration with PSRAM and single-threaded TCG, 477 s, §4.5). On a slower host, use `--timeout-scale` or `E2E_TIMEOUT_SCALE`. `SCENARIO_BUDGET_S` accepts one limit for each scenario, and `--scenario-timeout` sets one limit for all scenarios. `--timeout-scale` or `E2E_TIMEOUT_SCALE` multiplies the limit. When the limit expires, the harness dumps and kills the emulators of the scenario, and the scenario fails without a retry. After a fault or an expired limit, each wait of the harness (MQTT, console, HTTP, syslog) raises `AttemptAborted`. Thus a hung QEMU costs less than one minute, and not the sum of the *timeouts* of the scenario (before, approximately 47 minutes). The harness does not stop an operation that is not a harness wait (a `time.sleep`, an HTTP call in progress). `results.json` → `durations` keeps the duration of each attempt.

  ***Heap poisoning* (issue #104, item 3).** The QEMU build has `CONFIG_HEAP_POISONING_DISABLED`: freed memory keeps its value. Thus the #98 check (the interrupted download) would also pass with the old code, which read the handle after `esp_https_ota_abort()`. `E2E_POISON=1` compiles the same build with `sdkconfig.qemu-poison` on top (`CONFIG_HEAP_POISONING_COMPREHENSIVE`, directory `build-qemu-poison`). With this build, freed memory gets the value 0xFE, and each block has canaries that the allocator verifies when it frees the block. Test result: with the old sequence (`esp_https_ota_abort()` before the read of the length, a temporary change not in version control), the check fails with `interrupted after 4278124286 bytes` (0xFEFEFEFE). With the current code, the check passes, and `--only ota` passes completely (FW-1 in 115 s, the same as without *poisoning*). **#80 margins, without PSRAM, `--only tls`:** without *poisoning*, 137 064 B stay free after the worst case (minimum 13 568 B, largest free block 94 208 B), and the `PUT /api/config?dry_run=1` answers 200. With *poisoning*, 116 320 B stay free (approximately 20 KB less: the header and the canaries of each live block), and the largest free block is 73 716 B. The `PUT` then answers 503 (`memoria insuficiente para procesar la configuración`), and the #80 check fails. For this reason, this build is not in the main variant, and it is not in the PSRAM variant without a measurement. It has its own CI job with `--only ota` (`e2e-poison`). **Known limitation of that job:** with *heap poisoning*, one time at the boot of a trial image, the `LoadStorePIFAddrError` of the core 1 *tick* on SYSTIMER occurred without an image write before it (the read of the other slot in `gw_fw_early_init()`). Since the third revision, that fault does not cause a retry, because there is no evidence that it is not the firmware. If it occurs again, `e2e-poison` becomes red: read its `uart0-*.log`.

  **Self-tests.** `--simulate-qemu-death` tests each path. These cases cause one retry and pass:
  - `ota`: SIGABRT to the emulator in the OTA scenario. It also leaves an observer subscribed to the main broker (#105).
  - `boot`: SIGSEGV to the second emulator, 3 s after its start.
  - `console`: SIGSEGV to the first emulator before it connects the console (#96).
  - `main`: SIGABRT in `main` after the broker change.

  These cases cause no retry, and the run fails:
  - `hang`: a guest pause with `stop` through the QEMU monitor, immediately after its first `Take trusted digest key(s)`. The harness detects it, dumps it with the monitor responsive, and does not repeat it.
  - `hang-mute`: the same hang, with all of QEMU stopped by SIGSTOP.
  - `ota-failed`: a failed check before the death.
  - `ota-panic`: a firmware `StoreProhibited` *panic* on the console before the death.

  `tools/e2e/test_harness.py` (without QEMU, in the `host-tests` job) covers these items:
  - the known signature on the real log of #92, and its negative cases (PIF with the PC in IRAM without an image write, bootloader lines, a different exception, a different address);
  - each firmware fault of the list above, and the exception for the requested `panic`;
  - the dump analysis, and that a silence never causes a retry (also with the PC stationary in the ROM);
  - the retry rule and its limit;
  - the handover between attempts with a slow dump;
  - the monitor error;
  - the #105 cleanup;
  - the return of the Modbus slave to its state at the start of the attempt;
  - the port of the fake broker;
  - that each wait of the harness raises after a fault;
  - the crash diagnostics of QEMU and the TCG mode (#107);
  - the progress wait of the web uploads (#115);
  - the time window of a dropped RX frame, for the second measurement (#117).

  **Measured rate:** 4 runs of `--only ota` in the variant without PSRAM, with the retry: 6 scenario attempts, and QEMU ended 2 of them (SIGSEGV with no message, one during the *rollback* and one at the boot after the power cut). That is one attempt in three. All 4 runs passed with the retry. In the 9 full runs before #86, the OTA scenario failed because of QEMU in 3 runs. With one retry, the probability that a run becomes red because of this is approximately one in ten: it is not zero. The report is in `docs/upstream-reports.md` §6.
- **Public CA bundle, DS peripheral, microSD.** Not executed (§6.5, §6.5.1).

### 4.4 QA-2

`idf.py set-target esp32s3 build`: **0 warnings in the full build** (1219 targets). During the configuration, IDF v6.1 prints 5 CMake warnings of its own (`component_validation.cmake`: private directories of `esp_wifi` that `wpa_supplicant` uses). They do not come from the project or from the compiler.

The production build and its checks (`tools/check_production.sh`, issue #95) are on the branch `archive/efuse-production` (§3.7).

The components of the project compile with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`.

### 4.5 QA-4: CI

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on each push and pull request. It has these jobs:

| Job | What it does |
|---|---|
| `host-tests` | Runs the host tests (CTest). Decodes the `spb_dump` payloads with the official proto. Runs the self-test of the harness (`tools/e2e/test_harness.py`: the fault signatures of the emulator and the retry rule, #92/#96). Runs `pyflakes` on the Python tools, with no warnings (#107) |
| `firmware` | Runs `idf.py set-target esp32s3 build` in the `espressif/idf:v6.1` image, pinned by digest. Fails if a `warning:` occurs. Publishes the binaries and the `idf.py size` output as artifacts |
| `e2e-plan` | Issue #121. Reads the E2E groups from `tools/e2e/scenarios.py` (`CI_GROUPS`). Gives the matrix of the two next jobs |
| `e2e-build` | One job for each variant: `default` and `psram` (`E2E_PSRAM=1`, the octal PSRAM of QEMU, #85). Runs `run_e2e.sh` with `E2E_STAGE`. The script builds `build-qemu` or `build-qemu-psram`, and copies into one directory all the files that the harness needs: the merged flash image, the ELF, the two app images, the `sdkconfig`, `flash_args`, the test signing key and the version of `littlefs-python`. The `default` job also builds `build-qemu-plain` (the same for the two variants). Publishes the artifacts `e2e-images-default`, `e2e-images-psram` and `e2e-images-plain`. GitHub keeps them for 1 day, because they contain the throwaway signing key of the run |
| `e2e` | One job for each group and each variant (10 jobs). Installs QEMU, mosquitto and the harness packages. Gets the images of its variant, and `e2e-images-plain` when the group contains the OTA to NVS encryption (`GW42`). Runs `run_e2e.sh --only <group>` with `E2E_PREBUILT`: no build. In the `psram` jobs, the configuration, the Sparkplug engine, its payload buffer, mbedTLS and the *store & forward* ring are in PSRAM, as on the board. The GitHub containers do not permit `unshare`, so each job runs on the loopback interface of its container. Writes one line to the job summary: `N/N checks passed`, the retries and `qemu-exits.txt`. Publishes the logs, the payloads, `results.json`, `qemu-exits.txt` and the `fault-*.txt` dumps (#92, #96) as the artifact `e2e-<variant>-<group>`. Publishes `results.json` and `qemu-exits.txt` also as `e2e-result-<variant>-<group>` |
| `e2e-summary` | Gets all the `e2e-result-*` artifacts. Writes one table to the job summary, with one line for each group and a total for each variant. Fails in these conditions: a group has no `results.json` or a failed check; a group ran different scenarios from its `--only` list; a scenario ran in no group or in two groups; the groups of one variant repeated more scenarios than one full run can (`MAX_RETRIES`, 2) |
| `e2e-poison` | Issue #104 (item 3): `E2E_POISON=1 E2E_PLAIN=0 run_e2e.sh --only ota`. Runs the OTA scenarios on the build with full *heap poisoning* (`sdkconfig.qemu-poison`). This build overwrites the freed memory. Thus the use-after-free of #98 changes the reported number of bytes, and the check for it fails. It takes approximately 6–7 minutes of scenario, plus the build. Publishes the logs, `results.json`, `qemu-exits.txt`, the `fault-*.txt` dumps and `core-bt.txt`. It does not publish `payloads/` or `coredump-info.txt` |

**The E2E groups of the CI (issue #121).** Before, one job ran the full E2E of one variant. With PSRAM, that job took approximately 45 minutes. Now each group has its own job, and the groups run at the same time. `CI_GROUPS` in `tools/e2e/scenarios.py` gives the groups. The table shows the scenario time of a full local run, in seconds (PSRAM / default):

| Group | `--only` | PSRAM | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ota-wiring` | `network,ota-wiring` | 506 | 90 |
| `ota-fw1` | `ota-fw1` | 487 | 190 |
| `ota-trial` | `ota-park,ota-trial` | 408 | 132 |
| `nvs-main` | `nvs,main` | 402 | 401 |
| `other` | `failsafe,web,reset,tls,failover,mqtt5,phost,sf,https,slow` | 438 | 440 |

- The longest group with PSRAM is 506 s, near the longest scenario (the #97 integration, 496 s). Each job also boots its container and installs QEMU, and the build jobs come first.
- `ota-wiring` also contains `network`. The #97 integration has no MQTT observer, and the check "6. payloads decode" of each run needs at least one payload.
- `test_harness.py` (`CiGroups`) checks that each scenario of a full run is in exactly one group, and that no group is empty. It also checks that each group has a scenario with an observer, and that the group of `GW42` gets the build without NVS encryption.
- The harness makes 5 checks one time per run, after the scenarios: "6. payloads decode", #105, #92, #111 and SPB-5. Thus each group adds 5 checks. The total line of `e2e-summary` also gives the count with these checks one time, as in one full run. On `main` 8673b36, this count was 178 in the default variant (§4.3).
- The limit of the retries is for one full run (`MAX_RETRIES`, 2 scenarios). Each group has this limit, and `e2e-summary` applies it again to all the groups of one variant.

**Local runs of the groups.** `tools/e2e/run_ci_groups.sh <dir> [group ...]` does the same steps as the CI on one machine. It builds the images of one variant one time (`E2E_STAGE`), and runs the groups from them (`E2E_PREBUILT`). `PARALLEL` (default 2) sets how many groups run at the same time. Each group runs in its own network namespace when the harness can make one. With all the groups, the script also does the checks of `e2e-summary`. A loaded host makes the guest slower, so do not set `PARALLEL` higher than the number of CPUs divided by 2.

**The duration of the CI E2E runs with single-threaded TCG in the OTA scenarios (issue #107).** The values are the sum of the scenarios. The measurements come from this container, with another E2E in parallel:

- Without PSRAM: from 966 s to 1193 s (approximately 4 minutes more). FW-1 OTA 98 → 172 s, the integration test of #97 55 → 80 s, the test of #99/#101 69 → 103 s, the OTA to encrypted NVS 73 → 128 s.
- With PSRAM: from 992 s to 2030 s (approximately 17 minutes more). The same four scenarios take 307, 477, 386 and 131 s. The main cause is the web uploads of 1 MB. Each upload takes approximately 150 s, with AES and SHA in software.

If the `psram` jobs are too long, set `E2E_TCG_THREAD=multi` in the `e2e` job. This gives the previous times again, but QEMU deaths occur, and the harness retries them.

## 5. Status of the MVP IDs

Legend for **Evidence**:

- **H**: host test.
- **Q**: E2E in QEMU.
- **C**: compiled only, not run.

| ID | Status | Main file | Evidence / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| NET-1 | Implemented | `components/gw_net/gw_net.c` | C for the W5500; Q for the esp_netif/event path (with openeth). esp_eth reconnects the link |
| NET-2 | Implemented | `components/gw_net/gw_net.c` (`configure_ip`) | Q for DHCP; C for the static IP and the manual DNS |
| NET-3 | Implemented | `components/gw_edge/gw_edge.c` (hostname → esp-mqtt/getaddrinfo) | C: the E2E uses a literal IP address. It does not use DNS |
| TIME-1 | Implemented | `components/gw_net/gw_net.c`, `gw_edge.c` | Q: the UTC timestamps are less than 30 ms from the host time |
| MQTT-1 | Implemented | `components/gw_edge/gw_edge.c` | Q (host, port, client_id = edge_node_id; keepalive: the gateway detects a frozen broker in 1 × keepalive); user name and password C |
| MQTT-2 | Implemented | `gw_edge.c`, `gw_core/src/gw_backoff.c` | H + Q (broker restart and frozen broker: NDEATH, new bdSeq, NBIRTH and all the DBIRTHs; a broker that cuts the connection after the CONNACK: the backoff continues to increase) |
| MQTT-3 | Implemented | `gw_edge.c` (`prepare_attempt`) | Q |
| MQTT-7 | Implemented | `gw_edge.c`, `gw_bus.c` | Static queues and buffers, `outbox.limit` and `SKIP_PUBLISH_IF_DISCONNECTED`. No stress test |
| SPB-1 | Implemented | `gw_core/src/spb.c`, `proto/` | H + Python interoperability + Q |
| SPB-2 | Implemented | `gw_node.c`, `gw_edge.c` (`op_reboot`) | H + Q |
| SPB-3 | Implemented | `gw_storage/gw_storage.c`, `gw_edge.c` | Q (per attempt, and persistent) |
| SPB-4 | Implemented | `gw_core/src/gw_node.c` (`publish_nbirth`) | H + Q. Node metrics: bdSeq, Rebirth, Reboot, `Node Info/Firmware`, `Hardware`, `Reset Reason`, `Node Control/Scan Rate` (alias 1 and 3–7), DI/1–8, DO/1–8 and `Counter/1`–`8` (IO-4, alias 30–37) |
| SPB-5 | Implemented | `gw_node.c` | H (wrap from 255 to 0) + Q (continuity) |
| SPB-6 | Implemented | `gw_node.c` (`gw_node_device_changed`) | H + Q |
| SPB-7 | Implemented | `gw_node.c`, `gw_rbe.c` | H + Q. NDATA and DDATA have no datatype, also on the members of a template instance (`tck-id-payloads-metric-datatype-not-req`, #159). The births have the datatype of each metric |
| SPB-8 | Implemented | `gw_node.h` (alias map) | H + Q |
| SPB-9 | Implemented | `gw_node.c` (`gw_node_rebirth`) | H + Q |
| SPB-10 | Implemented | `gw_node.c`, `gw_poll.c` (`gw_poller_write`) | H + Q. The commands are all or nothing. An NCMD/DCMD that does not decode fully applies no metric (H + Q). A DCMD with writes that do not all fit in the bus queue also applies no metric (H). The gateway rejects a DCMD metric with a datatype that is not the datatype of the DBIRTH, and a value with a fraction for an integer or Boolean tag (#163, H). The log gives the reason. A metric without a datatype uses the datatype of the DBIRTH |
| SPB-11 | Implemented | `gw_node.c`, `gw_board/gw_io.c` | H + Q (simulated DO with the polarity of the board; TCA9554 C) |
| SPB-12 | Implemented | `gw_rbe.c` | H + Q |
| MB-1 | Implemented | `components/gw_rs485/gw_rs485.c` | Q at 9600 8N1; other parities C. The wait for the end of the transmission is proportional to the frame (the bits per character from the configuration + 20 ms): H at 1200 baud with parity |
| MB-2 | Implemented | `gw_rs485.c` (`UART_MODE_RS485_HALF_DUPLEX`) | **C**: QEMU does not support this mode. The RTS/DE timing has no verification. The UART ISR is in IRAM (`CONFIG_UART_ISR_IN_IRAM`) |
| MB-3 | Implemented | `gw_core/src/mb_rtu.c`, `gw_poll.c` | H (FC01–04) + Q (FC01, FC03, FC04) |
| MB-4 | Implemented | `mb_rtu.c`, `gw_poll.c` | H (all four) + Q (FC05, FC06 and a 32-bit FC16: one write and the DDATA). FC15/FC16 for 16 bits with `CONFIG_GW_MB_FORCE_MULTI_WRITE`. The gateway rejects NaN and infinite values, also for coils |
| MB-5 | Implemented | `gw_core/src/gw_conv.c` | H + Q (float32, int16, uint16, bool; with MB-6 also int32 in `BADC`) |
| MB-7 | Implemented | `gw_conv.c` | H + Q |
| MB-8 | Implemented | `gw_core/src/mb_master.c` | H + Q |
| MB-9 | Implemented | `gw_devstate.c`, `gw_poll.c` | H (also a block with no response while a different block responds; backoff polling of the offline devices; a healthy device that keeps its period next to three dead devices) + Q (three dead slaves polled at 2, 4, 8, 10 s, with no delay to the healthy device) |
| MB-10 | Implemented | `gw_poll.c` (`gw_poller_next`) | H + Q |
| MB-16 | Implemented | `components/gw_bus/gw_bus.c` | Design: one task and one queue. The DCMD writes occur between the requests of a cycle, always from the same task (MB-14) |
| IO-1 | Implemented | `components/gw_board/gw_io.c`, `gw_debounce.c`, `gw_bsp` | H (debounce and polarity) + Q (simulated pins with the polarity of `gw_bsp`); real GPIOs C |
| IO-2 | Implemented | `gw_core/src/gw_dout.c`, `gw_io.c`, `gw_bsp` | H (boot sequence with POLARITY = 0, active low, mixed values, bus errors, retry with backoff, and DO `is_null` while the expander is missing) + Q (simulated registers); real I²C C |
| IO-5 | Implemented | `components/gw_board/gw_status.c` | Q (states in the log); RMT/WS2812 C; the colors have no verification |
| CFG-1 | Implemented | `gw_core/src/gw_config.c`, `gw_storage/gw_storage.c`, `main/main.c` | H (maximum document of 256 tags, out of memory, JSON paths, line and column, duplicate keys) + Q (a file that is too large, empty, or has a syntax or schema error, and a partition that is not mounted: nothing starts, error LED, ERROR log with the cause) |
| CFG-2 | Implemented | `gw_storage/gw_storage.c`, `main/CMakeLists.txt` | Q (LittleFS; the embedded fallback only if the file is missing) |
| FW-4 | Implemented | `idf/CMakeLists.txt` (`PROJECT_VER`) | Q (`0.1.0+g<hash>[.dirty]` in the NBIRTH) |
| REL-1 | Implemented | `gw_bus.c`, `gw_edge.c`, `gw_io.c`, `sdkconfig.defaults` | Q: the firmware feeds the TWDT after each publish (the log of the births gives the longest interval without a feed). The TWDT also monitors the DIs as a user. A *panic* keeps the *core dump* in flash, and the gateway restarts (`Node Info/Reset Reason` = `panic`). `esp_coredump` decodes the dump. Issue #132: `Node Info/Core Dump`, `/api/coredump` (download, summary, erase) and the shell's `coredump` give the dump without USB (§3.2). H (`test_cdump`, `test_node`) + Q (download, summary, base64 lines, erase). The tests did not trigger the real TWDT: QEMU disables the timer watchdog |
| REL-2 | **Partial** | All `gw_*` | The code of the project is static and does not allocate in the critical path. **lwIP and esp-mqtt allocate from the heap internally** (bounded, not static) |
| REL-3 | Implemented | `esp_log` in all modules | Q. With REL-4 the console does not change |
| QA-1 | Implemented | `test/host/` | 265 cases |
| QA-2 | Implemented | — | 0 warnings; size in §3.2 |

**Outside the MVP**, fully or partially implemented:

- **MB-6** Implemented: the types `uint64`, `int64`, `float64` and `string` (1–16 registers), the orders `ABCD`/`CDAB`/`BADC`/`DCBA` (and also `big`/`little`), and `bit` 0–15 in a `holding`/`input` register. A bit is also writable (read, modify and write of the register). The 64-bit integers stay **exact** from end to end. The internal value is a union (double, int64, uint64 or text, as the tag sets), not a `double`. In Sparkplug, they go as UInt64/Int64 in `long_value`, `float64` goes as Double and `string` as String. Report by exception compares the 64 bits (or a hash of the text).

  Evidence: H (conversion in the four orders, 2^53 + 1, limits, texts, schema, polling, bit write with read-back, exact DCMD, DBIRTH/DDATA) + Python interoperability + Q (DBIRTH with the seven new tags in their orders; DCMD int64 2^62 + 3 with an FC16 of 4 registers; bit and text written).
- **MB-11** Implemented: the gateway reads contiguous or near tags of the same area in one block (up to 125 registers or 2000 bits). Each device can set `block_reads` (it disables the block reads) and `max_gap` (the maximum gap). Without them, the build option applies (`CONFIG_GW_MB_BLOCK_READS`, `CONFIG_GW_MB_BLOCK_MAX_GAP`; the default is block reads with gap 0). Evidence: H (three devices with different gaps and one with no block reads; schema) + Q (Bulk: 60 registers in one FC03 with the firmware gap 0; Meter1 with `max_gap: 10`: holding 10–37 in one read).
- **MB-12** Implemented: `scan: slow` per tag and `slow_poll_ms` per device (the default is 10 × `poll_ms`). The planner makes blocks for each class. Each cycle reads the fast blocks, plus the slow blocks when they are due. With the offline detection, the partial cycles count for `offline_after`, and only a full cycle (all the blocks) puts the device online. When a device is offline, each cycle is full. Evidence: H (classes, separate blocks, full cycle on return, slow blocks pending after a failure, a device with only slow tags) + Q (Energy and Serial every 3 s, the other devices every 1 s).
- **SPB-15** Implemented: `Node Control/Scan Rate` (UInt32, ms, alias 7) in the NBIRTH, writable by NCMD. **Semantics: it replaces, it does not scale.** 0 (the value at boot) = each device uses its `poll_ms`. A value from 50 to 3 600 000 ms replaces the `poll_ms` of **all** the devices. It also replaces their `slow_poll_ms` if that value is smaller (MB-12). A shorter period applies immediately. A longer period applies after the poll that is already scheduled.

  The offline devices do their backoff from the current period. The gateway discards a value that is out of range or not an integer, and writes a log line. In the two cases, an NDATA confirms the current value. **The gateway does not persist it:** after a restart, the value goes back to 0. The configuration is the source of truth: a value stored separately would make the gateway behave differently from `config.json`, and each NCMD would wear the flash.

  Evidence: H (metric, range, confirmation, Int64 by alias, rebirth; the schedule with the value, return to 0, backoff and slow scan) + Q (NCMD 3000 → Meter1 every 3 s and NDATA; invalid value rejected; 0 → 1 s again).
- **MB-15** Implemented: a Modbus TCP gateway (`modbus_tcp`, **off by default**). It translates MBAP → RTU (FC01–06, FC15, FC16). It sends each request through the task that owns the bus. That task serves the request between two polling requests, after the pending DCMD writes, one request at a time. The gateway itself answers these cases: the functions that it does not forward (exception 01), the data that is not valid (03), the units 0 and 248–255 (0A) and the units that do not respond (0B). Access control: `allowed_clients` (IPv4 addresses or CIDR networks; empty = any client, with a warning at boot).

  Evidence: H (frames, exceptions, reads and writes against the simulated slave, client list, schema) + Q (a pymodbus client reads holding 10 and writes holding 200 of unit 1 through port 502, which slirp forwards; the dead unit 3 gives 0B; FC 0x2B gives 01 and does not touch the bus; the gateway disconnects a client that is not in the list, with no response).
- **MB-13** Implemented: the gateway decodes the exceptions and writes them to the log. The counters per device (cycles, failed cycles, and attempts ok / timeout / CRC / frame / exception) are in the shell, in the offline log and, with SPB-17, in Sparkplug.
- **SPB-17** Implemented: `Node Info/Uptime` (UInt64, s), `Free Heap` and `Min Free Heap` (UInt32, internal RAM), `RSSI` (Int32, only with Wi-Fi configured; `null` with no association) and the DataSet `Node Info/Modbus`. The DataSet has one row per device with the MB-13 counters, also for the offline devices. These metrics are in the NBIRTH, and in an NDATA every `sparkplug.diagnostics_s` (60 s by default; the table only if it changed). This is the real use of the SPB-19 DataSet. H (NBIRTH, period, table only on changes, null RSSI, lost session, `diagnostics_s` 0) + Q (NBIRTH with the metrics, NDATA every 10 s with the uptime and the counters of Meter1 and Dead3) + C (RSSI: QEMU has no radio).
- **CFG-3** Implemented: an embedded web UI (one page, with no external resources). It has these items:
  - live status (`/api/status`: mode, interfaces, MQTT, devices with counters and values (exact 64-bit integers, texts and bytes), DI/DO (`null` if the expander does not respond), pulse counters, failsafe, DO with a local rule, buzzer alarm, time and RTC (#75), heap);
  - forms for network, MQTT, Sparkplug, RS485, credentials and devices/tags. Issue #167: a save from the forms with no change sends the configuration unchanged. The lists (parity, area, type, word order) have all the values of `gw_config.c`, and the host test `check_web_lists` compares them. A value that is not in a list stays selected, as an option of its own. An absent optional key (word order, parity, a checkbox with its default) stays absent. The tag table also has the `bit` and `length` columns. The other tag keys (for example `scan`) are only in the JSON tab, and a save from the forms keeps them;
  - a JSON editor, and validate, save, import, export and restart;
  - Basic auth with a PBKDF2 hash;
  - #170, #172: the Sparkplug card shows `sparkplug.device_prefix` as a list of levels, with buttons to add and remove a level, and a select for `sparkplug.naming`. Each device shows its Sparkplug topic (`spBv1.0/<group_id>/DDATA/<edge_node_id>/<device_id>`, with the length of the `device_id` in bytes) and its UMH topic (`umh.v1.<location>._raw.<first tag>`), with the name rules of UMH Core (`docs/reference/umh-names-and-topics.md`). A location level that starts with `_` shows in red. The previews change while the user types. The names and IDs have `maxlength` (39 for device names, types and tag names, 32 for the Sparkplug IDs, 15 for `units`), and `primary_host_id`, `type`, `naming` and the `bytes` type have short help texts. After a validation or a save, the page shows the warnings of #169 and marks their fields in amber. All this is JavaScript in the page: no RAM in the gateway.

  The web UI also starts with the rejected configuration (recovery mode) and in commissioning. Evidence: H (`gw_auth`: SHA-256 vectors, HMAC RFC 4231, PBKDF2 RFC 7914, Basic; `check_web_lists`) + Q (page, status, the lists of the served page hold the `float64`, `CDAB`, `DCBA` and `BADC` values of the configuration and the unchanged export is valid (#167), 401, recovery mode in the five cases of a rejected file, a correction through the API that starts the gateway). HTTPS with a certificate of the gateway, and a limit of attempts per address (issue #64, §6.2.1): H (`gw_authlimit`: lockout, doubling, cap, full table) + Q (the fingerprint on the console is the same as that of the certificate that the server gives, HTTP only redirects, 5 failures → 429 also with the correct password, counters in the shell, access after the lockout).
- **CFG-4** Implemented: a JSON REST API (`GET/PUT/POST /api/config`, `?dry_run=1`, `?download=1`, `POST /api/restart`, `POST /api/rebirth`). It gives a validation with the JSON path, or the line and column, of the error. It does an atomic write and an orderly restart (NDEATH). It masks the secrets on export. Writes need `X-Requested-With` and the same origin (CSRF, issue #100). H (`gw_cfgdoc`, error fields, depth) + Q (401, masked export, 400 with path, 400 with line, dry run, 413, a valid PUT → NDEATH and NBIRTH with the new configuration, a masked secret keeps its value). Issue #171: `POST /api/rebirth` (and the Rebirth button of the Status tab) sends the NBIRTH and all the DBIRTH messages again with the same bdSeq, through the same request flag as the shell `rebirth`. It gives `409` without a Sparkplug session. Q (NBIRTH with `seq` 0 and the bdSeq of the session, the DBIRTH messages, no rebirth and `403` without the CSRF header).
- **CFG-5** Implemented: factory reset with BOOT for 10 s (gestures in `gw_button`, host-tested). It erases all of LittleFS and the NVS. Then it starts commissioning mode without the example identity. H + Q (simulated button: a 3 s gesture does not reset, reset at 10 s, NDEATH, commissioning, the old password rejected, configuration loaded through the API, no old block in the partition). Real GPIO0, LED and real buzzer: C (the beeps: Q in the log).
- **CFG-6** Implemented: a shell with these commands:
  - `status`, `devices`;
  - `config` (a summary; per device: the fast and slow periods, the tags per scan class, the block reads with their gap; and a `modbus_tcp` line, #68);
  - `config show` (masked secrets);
  - `config load` / `config check` (paste a JSON: validate, save atomically and restart);
  - `restart`, `rebirth`, `mbread` (a Modbus read through the bus owner) and `ping`.

  The responses are atomic relative to the log (#56). The shell works with the rejected configuration. Q (show, mbread of a register, float32 and an absent device, rebirth, a rejected load with the path, check, a valid load that restarts). The shell has no password (physical access). `CONFIG_GW_SHELL_WRITE_COMMANDS=n` makes it read-only.

  **Modbus test read in the web UI (issue #182).** The configuration tab has a form for one read, and the REST API has `POST /api/modbus/read` (SPEC §6.1). Thus at commissioning, the operator can find out if a slave answers before the tags are configured, without the serial console. The read goes through `gw_bus_diag_read`, as `mbread` does: the bus task runs it between two poll transactions (MB-16). The request uses the static slot of the bus, so the bus path has no allocation. The web server task waits for the answer: a maximum of 2 s + 2 × `response_timeout_ms` × (`retries` + 1). One test read runs at a time (shell or web); a second one gets `503`. The count has a limit of 16 registers or bits. An optional `type` and `word_order` add the converted value (`gw_conv`, the same conversion as the tags). `gw_mbdiag` (gw_core) parses the request and writes the answer, with a host test (`test_mbdiag`). In recovery and commissioning modes, the bus does not run, and the endpoint gives `409` with the reason. The read is read only. Q (holding 10 of unit 1 = 50, input 0 as `float32` = 230.5, unit 3 gives no value, a count of 17 gives `400`, no CSRF header gives `403`, `409` in recovery).
- **CFG-7** Implemented: the secrets are outside the JSON, in NVS.
  - The NVS is encrypted with the HMAC key in eFuse KEY5 only in QEMU. It is not encrypted in the default build. The encryption with a key in eFuse on the board is out of scope (issue #133, §3.7).
  - Migration of a file with plaintext secrets (with an erase of the partition), and of an unencrypted NVS to the encrypted NVS (issue #67). The migration is safe against cuts. It does not occur while a new image is in its trial period (issue #94).
  - On a migrated gateway, the gateway rejects an OTA to an image without NVS encryption (issue #102).
  - The combination of encrypted NVS without flash encryption does not configure outside the emulator (issue #103).

  Evidence: H (`test_nvsraw`, with images from `nvs_partition_gen.py`: mixed partition, a u8 entry from the raw pages, the migration copy, the boot decision; `test_ota`: the capabilities block; `sdkconfig_check_nvsenc_*`) + Q (QEMU emulates HMAC and eFuse: encrypted NVS; no plaintext secret in the full flash image after the migration; an unencrypted NVS migrated, also with two cuts in the middle, and a boot without LittleFS after each cut; an OTA from the unencrypted build to the encrypted build with a *rollback*, and then confirmed; the return to the unencrypted build rejected, with the NVS intact).
- **NET-5** Implemented, **not tested with a radio**: an access point `GW-Setup-XXXXXX` (WPA2, commissioning password) when there is no valid configuration, and with BOOT for 2–10 s. Q (the two triggers reach `gw_ap`; the QEMU build has no radio) + C (SoftAP, AP+STA).
- **NET-6** Implemented: `network.hostname` (or a name derived from `edge_node_id`) in DHCP, and in mDNS with `_https._tcp` (or `_http._tcp` with `web.https: false`). H (schema, derivation) + Q (the responder starts with the derived name) + C (mDNS responses: `open_eth` has no multicast filter, §6.2.1).
- **MQTT-4** Implemented: TLS 1.2 and 1.3 to the broker (`mqtt.tls`, SPEC §6.1). The CA comes from a PEM file in LittleFS (`ca_file`) or from the ESP-IDF bundle of public root CAs. The gateway always verifies the name (the `host`, also an IP literal against an IP SAN, or `server_name`). It always verifies the dates (it waits for SNTP). The gateway validates the file at boot. If the file is missing or is not PEM, the gateway reports it and does not try to connect. The mbedTLS memory is in PSRAM if there is PSRAM.

  Evidence: H (schema) + Q (TLS 1.3 with the CA from LittleFS and the name against the IP SAN; a `server_name` that the certificate does not contain → rejected; CA missing → error and no attempts) + C (bundle of public CAs).
- **MQTT-5** Implemented: mutual TLS with a PEM client certificate and key in LittleFS (`mqtt.tls.cert_file`, `key_file`). The gateway validates them at boot. H (schema) + Q (a TLS 1.2 listener that requires a client certificate: NBIRTH, and mosquitto uses the CN of the certificate as the user name; without a certificate, the broker rejects the connection). The DS peripheral (a key out of reach of the firmware) is out of scope, because it burns an eFuse (§6.5.1).
- **SPB-14** Implemented: with `sparkplug.store_forward`, the gateway keeps the changes that occur without a Sparkplug session (broker down or primary host offline). It applies the exception and *deadband* rules. It keeps the changes in a circular buffer allocated at boot (40 000 samples in PSRAM; 512 in internal RAM without PSRAM). After the NBIRTH and the DBIRTHs, it publishes them from the oldest to the newest as DDATA/NDATA, with `is_historical` and their timestamp. It publishes 4 messages per loop. The logic is in `gw_sf` and `gw_node`.

  Evidence: H (ring, overflow, batches, history after the births, seq, a device without DBIRTH, a failed publish) + Q (broker stopped: three Voltage values and DI/3 on and off arrive as historical, in order, after the DBIRTH with the current value; internal ring, because QEMU has no PSRAM). The ring keeps exact 64-bit values and texts (continuation records).

  **Optional spill to microSD** (`store_forward.sd`, off by default, PSRAM required; the policy is in `gw_sfspill`). Since issue #124, the card holds a ring of self-checking blocks of 4 KB. When the RAM ring is three quarters full, its oldest samples go to the card, one block in each pass of the `sparkplug` loop. The gateway publishes them first. Evidence: H (a card simulation that injects torn writes, lost and damaged writes, and a power cut at each change: no damaged sample is published, the loss and the duplicates stay in their limits; the full card, a restart, a change of the configuration or of `max_kb`, a full card at the first use, both superblocks lost, a hole, the files of the earlier format, an I/O failure) + Q (`GW23`, PSRAM variant, with a LittleFS directory in place of the card: the spill and its replay, a power cut while offline, the delete of the files of the earlier format; the default variant: no spill without PSRAM) + C (SDMMC, FAT and the format on the board). Issue #136: before a planned restart, the gateway moves the RAM ring to the card, in a time limit of 5 s. Evidence: H (`test_sf`: the replay after the move, a slow card, a card failure) + Q (`GW23`, PSRAM variant: the shell `restart` and `POST /api/restart`) + C (the time of the move on the board, #131). Issue #139: the card is only for the gateway. The web UI and the shell format it in a task of their own, also a card that does not mount (exFAT, NTFS, ext4, GPT, no file system); `/api/status` shows the card, the spill and the format. Evidence: H (`test_cardfmt`) + Q (`GW23`: the refusal in QEMU, the auth and CSRF checks, the status objects, the page, and the format task with a simulated format) + C (the real format). See §6.5 and the GPIO45 risk.
- **SPB-13** Implemented: with `sparkplug.primary_host_id`, the gateway subscribes to the Sparkplug 3.0 STATE (retained JSON) together with NCMD/DCMD. It sends the NBIRTH only when the host is online. When the host goes offline, it sends an explicit NDEATH and disconnects. It ignores old STATE messages (smaller timestamp than the last one on the same broker, issue #157). With backup brokers, a valid offline STATE makes the gateway go to the next broker at once (issue #156). If no STATE `online` comes in 30 s, this counts as a failed attempt on that broker. The logic is in `gw_phost` (no IDF, no `malloc`).

  Evidence: H (strict parser, invalid payloads, timestamps and sessions, one timestamp for each broker) + Q (retained STATE `false` → no NBIRTH, and the shell tells this; `true` → NBIRTH; an old `false` → ignored; `false` → NDEATH with the bdSeq of the session and a wait in the next session; `true` → NBIRTH with a new bdSeq). See §6.3.
- **MQTT-8** Implemented: MQTT 5.0 by configuration (`mqtt.protocol: "5"`; 3.1.1 by default), with the Sparkplug 3.0 semantics on MQTT 5 (*Clean Start*, *Session Expiry Interval* 0, *Will Delay Interval* 0, and the reason code 0x04 in each DISCONNECT, issue #155). H (schema) + Q (CONNECT with protocol level 5 and *Clean Start*, as mosquitto reports; NBIRTH; NCMD Rebirth through the v5 subscription; NCMD Reboot gives the explicit NDEATH and the NDEATH of the *will* with the same bdSeq; the NDEATH of the *will* immediately when the connection stops). See §6.5.3.
- **MQTT-6** Implemented: an ordered list of brokers (`mqtt.backup_brokers`, up to 3 in addition to `mqtt.host`). The gateway goes to the next broker after `failover_after` consecutive failed attempts. An optional return to the primary is available (`primary_return_s`: one attempt after N s of a stable session on a backup). The logic is in `gw_brokersel` (no IDF). H (order, circular wrap, a stable session that keeps the broker, return to the primary with one attempt, schema) + Q (primary down at boot → backup; primary available again → NDEATH on the backup and NBIRTH on the primary after keepalive + 10 s; primary down again → backup).
- **MB-14** Implemented: the bus task serves the DCMD write queue before each cycle **and between two requests of a cycle** (the `between_requests` hook of `gw_poll`). A write waits at most for the current transaction. It does not wait for the remainder of the cycle, or for the cycles of other devices. A write can go to a tag of the device that the gateway polls at that time. Then the confirmed value also replaces the working copy of the cycle (the gateway does not publish the old value that it read before). Evidence: H (a cycle of 10 requests: the write goes out at the next boundary, a latency of one transaction, the confirmed value published) + Q (with responses of 120 ms, a DCMD in the middle of a cycle goes out between requests of the same cycle).
- **SPB-16** Implemented: the properties `engUnit`, `engLow`, `engHigh` (the tag keys `units`, `eng_low`, `eng_high`) and `Quality` (0 BAD / 192 GOOD) in the `PropertySet` of each metric. The codec uses nanopb callbacks (no memory proportional to the quantity). The DBIRTH has all the properties. The DDATA has `Quality` only when it changes. The node DOs are BAD while the expander does not respond. The worst DBIRTH (64 tags of 39 characters with all the properties) is approximately 10 KB. The payload buffer is 16 KB with PSRAM (`GW_SPB_PAYLOAD_MAX`). Without PSRAM, it is `CONFIG_GW_SPB_PAYLOAD_INTERNAL`, 6 KB by default (§1.3).

  Evidence: H (codec, wire format, truncated keys, worst case, engine) + Q (DBIRTH with the properties of `Voltage`; a read with exception 02 → `is_null` and BAD, and back to GOOD) + verification with the official proto.
- **#81** Implemented: the DataSet `Node Info/Modbus TCP` (alias 56) with the counters per unit of the forwarded Modbus TCP requests (#69), and one row for the units without a device. It is present only with `modbus_tcp.enabled`. The gateway publishes it with the polling table. H (NBIRTH, NDATA with only one changed TCP counter, the worst NBIRTH of 13.4 KB) + verification with the official proto + Q.
- **SPB-18** Implemented: an optional `type` key per device. Each type is a Template definition in the NBIRTH (no alias; members with name, datatype, default value and engineering properties). Each device with a type publishes an instance in the DBIRTH (alias 100 + its first tag; members by name with value and `Quality`). In the DDATA, it publishes only the changed members. DCMD per member of the instance (or by the separate name). The memory is bounded: callbacks produce the members (no tables), there is one level only, and the definitions describe 64 tags at most (worst-case NBIRTH ≈ 10 KB, test).

  Evidence: H (codec, engine, validation, worst case) + verification with the official proto + Q (two pumps of the `Pump` type at the boot of GW02: definition, instances, DCMD → FC06 → DDATA).
- **SPB-19** Implemented: DataSet, Bytes and File in the codec (encode and decode). DataSet through callbacks: a function produces the cells during the encode, and a different function receives them during the decode (no table in memory). Numeric columns, Boolean, String and Text. Bytes and File go in `bytes_value`, up to 64 B. Real use: the `bytes` tag type (a block of 1–16 registers as Bytes, writable by DCMD with FC16) and the DataSet `Node Info/Modbus` (SPB-17).

  Evidence: H (round trip of the three, format, limits, `bytes` tag in the engine) + verification with the official proto (DataSet with all the column classes, File) + Q (`bytes` tag in the DBIRTH, and DCMD Bytes → FC16 → DDATA, at the boot of the GW02 node).
- **IO-3** Implemented: a safe state per output (`local_io.do_failsafe`: `off`, `on` or `hold`), at restart and after `local_io.failsafe_s` without a Sparkplug session (SPEC §6.1). Before the gateway loads the configuration, all the outputs go to OFF. The exceptions are the `hold` outputs of the last valid configuration (mask in NVS). These outputs keep the TCA9554 latch if the restart was a warm restart (their pins were already outputs). With the rejected configuration, all the outputs go to OFF. Only one task (`local_io`) writes the expander.

  Evidence: H (`gw_dout`: read of CONFIG and OUTPUT before the write, latch kept only with output pins, cold boot, failed read; `gw_localio`: boot state, one trigger N s after the boot or the loss, rearm, clock wrap; schema) + Q (NBIRTH with the `on` output on at boot; broker down for 9 s with `failsafe_s` 5: the trigger at 5 s, the next NBIRTH with the safe states; `hold` kept after NCMD Reboot and off after a cut, with the simulated expander in RTC memory) + C (real I²C).
- **IO-6** Implemented: a buzzer on GPIO46 (LEDC; the firmware configures it only in `app_main`, because it is a *strapping* pin). It has configurable alarms (`local_io.buzzer`: `config_error`, `mqtt_down`, `device_offline`, with `alarm_after_s`). It also gives the beeps of the BOOT button: 2 when the user confirms the factory reset (CFG-5; `gw_buzzer_beep_wait`, before the erase) and 1 for the access point. Only one task controls the buzzer. The button only sends beep requests to it.

  Evidence: H (`gw_buzz`: requested beeps, delay, priority, period of 10 s, a request before the alarm burst, silent buzzer; schema) + Q (in the log of the QEMU build: `device offline` 5 s after the boot, `MQTT down` 5 s after the loss of the session and then `device offline` again, `configuration error` in recovery mode as NVS records it, 1 and 2 beeps of the button) + C (real LEDC and GPIO46).
- **IO-4** Implemented: an activation counter per DI (the debounce edges, up to approximately 25 Hz with 20 ms). The metrics `Counter/1`…`Counter/8` are UInt64, in the NBIRTH and in the NDATA of the change. The gateway stores them in NVS every `local_io.counter_save_s` (600 s by default) if they changed, and before each orderly restart (*shutdown handler*). It restores them at boot. It does not use PCNT or the RAM of the PCF85063 (one byte only).

  Evidence: H (edge count, activations only, exact UInt64 above 2^53, NBIRTH and NDATA, save schedule, schema) + Q (NBIRTH with 8 counters, NDATA with DI/1 and `Counter/1` = 1, a periodic save that stays after a cut, a save at NCMD Reboot).
- **IO-7** Implemented: local DI→DO rules (`local_io.rules`, up to 8, one per DO): `follow`, `invert` and `toggle`. The `toggle` rule counts the activations with the IO-4 counters, so it does not lose an activation between two evaluations. The `local_io` task evaluates the rules. This task is the only owner of the TCA9554, and the DI sampling wakes it. Thus the rules operate without a network.

  **Priorities:** the rule owns a DO that has a rule. The gateway rejects an NCMD on it with a log line and an NDATA of the real state. The safe state on MQTT loss does not touch it. At restart, the DO starts in its `do_failsafe` until the first evaluation (the validation rejects `on`).

  Evidence: H (`follow`/`invert`/`toggle`, restored pulses that do not invert, an even number of activations, safe state without the DOs of the rules; schema: repeated DO, `on` with a rule, ranges) + Q (`di 2 1` → DO/5 in NDATA without a command, NCMD on DO/5 rejected, two activations of DI/3 → DO/6 on and off, with the broker down DI/3 sets DO/6 to on and the safe state keeps DO/5 and DO/6 as they are) + C (real I²C).
- **NET-4** Implemented, **not tested with a radio**: Wi-Fi STA as a backup (`role: backup`; Ethernet gets the route) or as primary (`role: primary`). It has *failback* by hysteresis (`failback_s`) and a new MQTT session (new bdSeq, NBIRTH) when the interface changes. Also (issue #57), an interface can keep its link and address, but no broker of the list responds through it. Then this interface becomes "no service" (60 s, doubled up to 30 min), and the session moves to the other interface.

  Evidence: H (`gw_netsel`: failover, failback, bounces, Ethernet only, an interface with no service with an increasing wait and last resort; schema `network.wifi`) + Q (a configuration with Wi-Fi in the build without a radio: a warning, and Ethernet as before; shell `status` with the active interface) + C (Wi-Fi driver, real switchover, socket binding).
- **NET-7** Implemented: optional IPv6 (`network.ipv6`) on all the interfaces. It has link-local at each link-up event, and SLAAC with the RAs (`CONFIG_LWIP_IPV6_AUTOCONFIG`, enabled per interface only if the configuration requests it). It also has a static address on Ethernet, and IPv6 DNS. An interface with only a global IPv6 address counts as usable. Evidence: H (IPv6 literals, schema) + Q (link-local, SLAAC on the `fec0::/64` of slirp, static `fec0::1234`, a response to `ping fec0::2`) + C (MQTT, SNTP and DNS on IPv6; see §6.6).
- **FW-1** Implemented: OTA through HTTPS (`esp_https_ota`; the CA from `ota.ca_file`, from `mqtt.tls.ca_file` or a public CA), or upload through the web to the other partition. The trigger is an NCMD (`Node Control/Firmware Update`) or the web (`POST /api/firmware`, Firmware card). The status and the progress are node metrics. The new image boots in its trial period. The gateway confirms the image with its first full Sparkplug session (all the DBIRTHs, issue #99), when it saves a configuration, or at a factory reset. The gateway retries a failed confirmation.

  A *rollback* occurs at the time limit, on a rejected configuration, when there is not enough memory to boot, and on a restart during the trial period (§3.6). A rejection during the trial period does not hide the trial (issue #101).

  Evidence: H (`test_ota`: policy, retried confirmation, incomplete session, operator, rejections, URLs, progress, request body; `test_node`: metrics and NCMD; `test_config`: the `ota` section) + Q (download, upload, rejections, confirmation, the two *rollback* cases, a session without one DBIRTH that does not confirm, and a saved configuration that confirms, §4.3). The *rollback* when there is not enough memory for the configuration: C (QEMU cannot cause it without a different build).
- **FW-2** Out of scope (issue #133): Secure Boot burns eFuses. The implementation is on the branch `archive/efuse-production` (§3.7). The QEMU build still requires signed OTA images without Secure Boot, and #133 decides whether this stays. Q (the gateway rejects an OTA signed with a different key, §4.3).
- **FW-3** Out of scope (issue #133): flash encryption burns eFuses. The implementation is on the branch `archive/efuse-production` (§3.7).
- **REL-4** Implemented (syslog: yes; logs through MQTT: no). The gateway copies each `esp_log` line to the console, and also to an RFC 5424 syslog server over UDP (`syslog.host`, `port`, `level`). The task that writes the log never waits. The gateway formats the line only if the format lock is free (*try-lock*). Then the line goes into a ring of `CONFIG_GW_SYSLOG_SLOTS` lines (in PSRAM if there is PSRAM). If not, the gateway discards and counts the line, and later the server receives a warning with the number.

  The gateway does not forward the lines of the sender task itself (no recursion). Evidence: H (parse of the `esp_log` lines, RFC 5424 format, timestamps, full ring, schema) + Q (lines with PRI, HOSTNAME, APP-NAME and the SNTP timestamp; a warning with the broker down; `sequenceId` with no gaps at each boot; counters in `status`).
- **TIME-2** Implemented: the gateway reads the PCF85063 at boot (OS bit, BCD and plausibility in `gw_rtc`, host-tested). It writes the PCF85063 after each SNTP update, through the only owner of the I²C bus. H (BCD, days from 2025 to 2099 against the libc, OS bit, invalid fields, out of range) + Q (simulated PCF85063: OS bit at power-on; after NCMD Reboot with SNTP silent, the RTC time and an NBIRTH with valid timestamps immediately) + C (real I²C).
- **TIME-3** Implemented: `Node Info/Time Synced` (Boolean, alias 55) in the NBIRTH, and in an NDATA when it changes. Only SNTP sets it to `true`. The RTC time is valid for the timestamps, but it can have drift. H + Q (`true` in the NBIRTH after SNTP, `false` in the NBIRTH with the RTC time, NDATA `true` when SNTP is available again).
- **REL-5** Implemented: `esp_reset_reason()` goes to the boot log and to the NBIRTH as `Node Info/Reset Reason` (String, alias 6). H + Q (`power-on` at the first boot, `software` after NCMD Reboot).
- **REL-6**: measured in QEMU.
- **QA-3** Partial: QEMU, no hardware. The harness detects a dead or hung QEMU and the known emulator faults, and dumps its state. It sets a time limit for each scenario. It retries only these faults, one time, if nothing failed before (#86, #90, #92, #96). It closes what an attempt leaves open (#105). A variant with *heap poisoning* is available for OTA (#104).
- **QA-4** Implemented: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (§4.5). It also includes the OTA scenarios with *heap poisoning* (#104).

## 6. Deviations and known limitations (as they are)

### 6.1 No hardware

These parts never ran:

- the W5500 driver and its SPI;
- the RS485 hardware mode (this includes the RTS/DE timing at 115200). The UART ISR is in IRAM (`CONFIG_UART_ISR_IN_IRAM`), so the DE releases in time also when the cache is disabled (for example, during an `nvs_commit`). Nobody has verified this with an oscilloscope;
- the real TCA9554;
- the real polarity of the DIs;
- the color order of the LED;
- the real octal PSRAM (QEMU runs its model: §4.3, #85), and the fault mode with a real defective PSRAM (the E2E removes the QEMU model: §1.3, #135);
- the boot with QIO flash;
- the Wi-Fi: association, reconnection, the switch between interfaces with two real links, and the binding of the MQTT socket (`SO_BINDTODEVICE`).

The QEMU build is different from the board build at these points (`sdkconfig.qemu`).

### 6.2 Configuration

- **An invalid or unreadable configuration = no polling and no MQTT.** The SPEC says "do not start the polling". Only the network, the web, the shell and the access point start, so you can correct the configuration without a new flash operation. The network uses the last valid configuration (the gateway saves it in NVS when it changes), or DHCP on Ethernet. If the network of the last valid configuration is not usable (for example, a static IP of a different plant), the access point or the shell stays available.
- **The embedded backup applies only when the file is missing.** A gateway flashed without the `storage` partition (for example, with only the app) does not mount LittleFS and starts in recovery mode (no polling, no MQTT). This is intentional: the gateway must never boot with the Sparkplug identity of the example.
- **Strict validation and our own decisions:**
  - unknown or duplicate keys = error;
  - `coil`/`discrete` ⇒ `bool`;
  - `scale` ≠ 0;
  - `bool` without `scale`/`offset`;
  - tag and device names ≤ 39 characters;
  - `group_id`/`edge_node_id` ≤ 32 characters, without `/ + #`;
  - #172: `sparkplug.device_prefix` ≤ 96 bytes, with the same characters as a Sparkplug ID and no empty level (no `:` at the start or the end, no `::`). The full `device_id` (prefix, `:` and name) must fit the longest device topic, `spBv1.0/<group_id>/DBIRTH/<edge_node_id>/<device_id>`, in 159 bytes. The check uses the real lengths: with a `group_id` and an `edge_node_id` of 32 bytes, the `device_id` can have 78 bytes. The validation does not limit the number of levels;
  - issue #162: the Sparkplug IDs, the device types, the tag names and `units` must be valid UTF-8 (RFC 3629: no overlong form, no surrogate). The Sparkplug IDs must also not contain U+0080–U+009F, because a broker can close the connection for a topic with a control character;
  - issue #162: two device names, two device types, or two tag names of one device must not differ only in case (`tck-id-case-sensitivity-sparkplug-ids`, `tck-id-case-sensitivity-metric-names`). The specification says SHOULD NOT, and the gateway rejects the configuration. The comparison folds only the ASCII letters: `É` and `é` are different names for the check;
  - issue #162: a tag name must not also be a folder of another tag of the same device (`A` and `A/B`, also `a` and `A/B`). A device type is an NBIRTH metric, so it must not be the name or the first folder of a node metric (`bdSeq`, `DI`, `DO`, `Counter`, `Node Control`, `Node Info`, `Firmware Update`). These checks stay errors, because they come from the Sparkplug specification;
  - #169: the names that UMH Core and other UNS hosts change are warnings, not errors (§6.3.0). With `sparkplug.naming: "umh"` they are errors;
  - ranges that we selected: `response_timeout_ms` 10–5000, `retries` 0–5, `poll_ms` ≥ 50, `keepalive_s` 5–3600, `inter_frame_delay_ms` ≤ 1000;
  - MB-6: `bool` in `holding`/`input` requires `bit`; `bit` does not accept a different `word_order`; `length` is necessary with `string` and `bytes`, and is not permitted with other types; `string` and `bytes` do not accept a `scale` other than 1, an `offset` or a `deadband` other than 0, `eng_low` or `eng_high`.

  The Zephyr implementation can be less strict: an agreement on this is necessary.
- **`units`**, `eng_low` and `eng_high` go only in the DBIRTH, as the properties `engUnit`, `engLow` and `engHigh` (SPB-16). `engLow`/`engHigh` go as Float for all tag types. A Float uses 4 B per value in the 256-tag configuration. This configuration is in memory two times while the gateway validates a new one (in internal RAM in the QEMU build without PSRAM). Thus the gateway rounds a range with more than 7 significant digits.

### 6.2.1 Web, API, shell and secrets (CFG-3 to CFG-7, NET-5, NET-6)

- **HTTPS with a self-signed certificate (issue #64).** No other party certifies the certificate of the gateway. The first time, the trust comes from the fingerprint (on the console). You cannot load a certificate of the plant (signed by its CA), and there is no renewal. The certificate is valid until 2099 and changes only with a factory reset. The key is in NVS with the secrets (encrypted in the production build; in plain text in the development build). The certificate has `CN=<hostname>` and the SANs `<hostname>.local` and `<hostname>`. It does not have the IP, because the IP can change with DHCP.
- **Memory for HTTPS.** Each TLS session of the server uses approximately 40 KB of mbedTLS memory (in PSRAM, §6.5.1). There are a maximum of 3 HTTPS connections at the same time. The stack of the server increased from 8 to 10 KB, because the handshake and the configuration validation run in the same task. The HTTP redirect runs in a second small server (3 KB of stack, 2 connections).
- **Limit of attempts per address (issue #64).** After 5 failures in sequence, the server sends 429 for 30 s. This time doubles at each new block, to a maximum of 15 min. A correct login clears the block. The server keeps 8 addresses (it uses again the oldest address that is not blocked). Many different addresses (or false addresses on the local network) can continue to try at that rate, each one. Behind a NAT, all clients share one address: a user that makes an error blocks the other users for some time. The log records the failures (`wrong credentials` with the address, `blocked for N s`). The shell also shows them (`web … logins failed N, refused while blocked N, blocks N`). The server keeps only the last accepted `Authorization` header (its SHA-256), so it does not calculate PBKDF2 again for each request.
- **CSRF (issue #100).** Each `PUT`/`POST` must have `X-Requested-With`. If the browser sends `Origin` (or, without it, `Referer`), the value must be this same gateway (scheme, `Host` and port). The server does this check before the password check. Thus a request from a different site does not test credentials and does not count as a failed login (a different site cannot block the operator). The server does not answer `OPTIONS` and does not send CORS headers: a different site cannot add the header. `POST /api/firmware` accepts only `application/json` (URL) and `application/octet-stream` (image).

  **Accepted names (issue #106):** the server compares `Host` and `Origin` (or `Referer`) in a normalized form. The name is in lowercase and without the final dot (`GW01.local.` = `gw01.local`). IPv6 is in brackets, in lowercase. The port is in decimal without leading zeros, and the server removes it if it is the port of the scheme or empty (`10.0.0.5:0443` = `10.0.0.5`). Any name of the gateway is valid (IP, `<hostname>.local`, `<hostname>`, a DNS name of the plant), if the page and the request use the same name. The IP in one and `gw.local` in the other are still two origins, and this is correct. The browser also sees two origins: it would send a *preflight* request because of the header, and the gateway does not answer it. Thus the page of the gateway never sends that request.

  The server rejects a malformed `Host` or `Origin` (spaces, `@`, two `:`, a port out of 1–65535, a bracket that is not closed). Limits: the server also rejects a `Referer` of more than 255 characters or an `Origin` of more than 127 characters.

  **DNS rebinding:** a site that makes its own name resolve to the IP of the gateway sends the same `Host` and `Origin`, so this check does not stop it. Two other items stop it. The certificate is not for that name, and the browser does not accept it without a user confirmation. The browser keeps the password per origin, and does not send it to that site. The server does not compare the `Host` with a list of its own names. `GET` requests do not change state, and the server does not check them (a different site can send them but cannot read the response). A custom client (script, `curl`) must add the header (SPEC §6.1). Nobody tested this in a real browser: the E2E sends the requests that a browser would send.
- **Bodies of rejected requests (issue #106).** `esp_http_server` ignores a `Connection: close` in the response. When the handler ends, it reads and discards the remainder of the body in 32 B pieces, in its single task. Before, a 1.5 MB `POST` without credentials or from a different site kept the web busy for the full upload. Anyone on the network could do this again and again.

  Now the server applies a special procedure to a **not trusted** rejection (401, 429, 403: credentials not checked yet, or a different site) with a body of more than 1 KB. The session replaces its receive function with a function that fails. The response goes out, and httpd stops the read immediately and closes the session in the same step. The server did not read the body, so the discard calls that function. The function fails and httpd deletes the session: no subsequent request can use it.

  For a rejection of an **authenticated, same-origin** request (400 invalid image, 409 busy, 413, 415), the server does **not** cut the body. httpd discards all of the body after the response, as before #106. Thus the web UI or a script that still sends data receives the code and the reason. (Review of #106: with a cut, a browser that uploaded 1.5 MB received a network error, and the page stayed at «Subiendo…».) The cost: an authenticated, same-origin client keeps the web busy for the duration of its upload. Each read of the discard waits up to `recv_wait_timeout` (5 s) if the client stops sending data. Also, the page now shows a network error and does not continue to wait (`fetch` with `catch` in all writes). After an image upload, the page then reads `/api/firmware` and shows its details.

  Measured in QEMU (E2E, `GW22`), with a 1.5 MB upload at 150 KB/s and a `GET` half a second later:
  - By HTTPS, with 401 without credentials and 403 from a different site: the `GET /api/status` responds in 0.3–0.4 s, and the upload stops at 48 KB. The client sees the 401/403 and then the cut connection.
  - By the HTTP port (redirect): 308, the `GET` in 0.02 s, and the upload stops at 32 KB.
  - Without the cut (a control test with the fix disabled), on the HTTP port the `GET` waited **11.5 s**, until the end of the upload.

  By HTTPS the `GET` did not wait also without the fix, and **the cause is not known**. The server sockets block with `SO_RCVTIMEO` (`recv_wait_timeout`, 5 s), and the esp-tls sockets also block. (The previous text of this paragraph said that they did not block; this was an error of the review.) By HTTPS, the result that we see is that the server does not read the body now. With `web.https: false`, the API uses a socket like the socket of the redirect.

  Limits: lwIP closes a socket that has unread data with a *reset*. Thus a client that continues to send can see a cut connection and not the code (a browser shows a network error). The server continues to discard bodies of up to 1 KB fully (they are almost always in the buffer already). Thus the client reads the response, and a 401 to a `GET` keeps the connection. The shell counts the cuts (`web … bodies cut N`). The counter is atomic: the tasks of the two servers increment it. For a client that sends the headers very slowly (*slowloris*), see the next item (issue #110).
- **The web is for a trusted network.** One client can keep the single task of `esp_http_server` busy while it sends its request. The web and the API are not designed for exposure to the Internet or to a network with hostile clients (use a VPN or a management segment). **What issue #110 limits, and only that: the *watchdog* decreases the time that a *slowloris* holds the web, to approximately 8 s per connection; it does not prevent it.**

  A dedicated low-priority task (`web_guard`, 2.5 KB static stack) does this work. It is not the `esp_timer` timer, because that timer also samples the DIs at 1 kHz and must not block. Every 1 s, the task asks each server, through its control socket, to mark that it returned to its loop. Our *handlers* set the mark when they start and when they end. The body discard of an authenticated rejection sets the mark with each piece that arrives.

  A server can go more than `CONFIG_GW_WEB_STALL_S` (8 s) without a mark while it is not in one of our *handlers* (an upload or a long validation). Then the task does a `shutdown()` on the sessions of that server. It does this only on the sockets whose local port is the port of the server, because the socket number can be in use again. Thus these items release the server task in that time: a TLS *handshake*, headers that arrive one byte at a time (*slowloris*), or a client that stopped in the middle of the request. The log records the event (`… server held for more than 8 s by a request that does not arrive`), and the shell counts it (`web … stalls N`).

  Measured in QEMU (E2E, `GW22`): a client sent one header byte each 2 s. This is less than the 5 s wait of each read, so httpd never releases the client. A normal `GET`, started 1.5 s later, responded in 8.9 s. With the *watchdog* disabled (control test), the `GET` waited 30.2 s, the full duration of the slow send.

  Limits:
  - The task also closes the idle sessions of the same server (the browser connects again).
  - An attacker that opens one slow connection after another can continue to hold the web, 8 s at a time.
  - There is no limit of connections per address. With 3 HTTPS sessions and `lru_purge_enable`, a new session replaces the oldest session. The block is on the task, not on the sessions.
  - A lower `recv_wait_timeout` does not help, because the time limit is per read and not per request.
- **In recovery mode, the stored web password is valid with any user name:** the user name is in the rejected document.
- **The shell starts first (issue #72).** The REPL starts immediately after NVS and the BOOT button, before the configuration loads (`shell: console ready`). The shell answers the commands that you type during the boot.
