The latency is the debounce, plus the wake-up of the `local_io` task, plus one I²C write (some milliseconds on the board, not measured). When the expander is down, the rules wait until it comes back.
- The polarity of the DOs (active low) comes from the demo and from ESPHome, without a schematic or a board. If a measurement shows the opposite, set `CONFIG_GW_DO_ACTIVE_LOW=n`.

### 6.10 Tests and environment

- **Crashes in a row in QEMU (issue #144).** The RTC memory of the boot-loop counter survives a panic reset in QEMU, so the E2E examines safe mode (scenario `ota-trial`). But the QEMU `open_eth` model keeps its state across a panic reset. In the first run, the boot after the second panic with traffic stopped in an endless `open_eth` interrupt (`emac_opencores_isr_handler`, core 0). Thus the E2E uses the emulator-only `panic net-off`: it stops the Ethernet driver, then aborts. The reset reason stays `panic`. On the board, a panic resets the W5500 driver at the next boot, so this limit does not apply. The rules of the counter for each reset reason are also in the host tests (`test_bootloop`).
- **The uptime of the self-check (issue #144).** In QEMU, the system timer of `esp_timer` does not restart at a software reset. Thus the self-check uses the FreeRTOS tick of the boot, not `esp_timer_get_time()`.

- **The connection that "did not arrive" at httpd (issue #110).** This occurred one time: an E2E `PUT` had no response in 15 s, and the gateway log showed no *handshake*. `--web-stress 90` in `--only https` with PSRAM reproduced it. This is 90 rounds, each with 6 cut sessions, 3 accepted `PUT`s, one rejected upload and one `GET`. The result was 2 failures in 90 rounds, both `The handshake operation timed out` on the client. No web *watchdog* triggered (the httpd task continued to serve requests).

  The gateway log showed `opencores.emac: RX frame dropped (0x14)` 44 times. The QEMU `open_eth` model has 4 DMA receive buffers. The slirp bursts during full-speed uploads overflow them. If the lost frame is the SYN of the next connection, slirp sends it again only after its exponential wait. The harness client (15 s) stops before that.

  The cause is not httpd, lwIP or the #106 cut. With 10 buffers (`CONFIG_ETH_OPENETH_DMA_RX_BUFFER_NUM=10`, the default value of the internal EMAC of the ESP32; the S3 has no EMAC), the same run gave 90/90 with no failures and 0 lost frames. This setting is **only in `sdkconfig.qemu-psram`**. The problem occurred in that build, and that build has sufficient internal RAM.

  In the build without PSRAM, the 9.6 KB increase decreased the largest free block at the #80 validation from approximately 29 KB to 18 432 B (minimum free 27 292 B). That is 1 KB from the 17 KB threshold, and it would make that measurement incorrect. The build without PSRAM keeps 4 buffers (issue #113). With 4 buffers, 60 `--web-stress` rounds lost 58 frames. There were no failures that time, but each lost frame can be a SYN.

  A test with 6 buffers gave 0 lost frames in the same 60 rounds. But the largest block at the #80 validation decreased to 24 576 B in that run, and to **18 432 B** in a full run (27 648 B with 4; threshold 17 KB). This was too near the threshold for the measurement of #80, which ran in the build without PSRAM before issue #135. Now the #80 check runs in the PSRAM variant, and the build without PSRAM keeps 4 buffers.

  The selected solution does not change the firmware. The web clients of the harness make the TCP connection and the TLS *handshake* in up to 3 attempts. The attempts share the *timeout* of the caller. Each attempt has at least 5 s, so a short *timeout* keeps its single attempt. The clients try again only after a *timeout*, and only before they send a byte of the request. Thus the request never repeats, and a server that does not accept within the *timeout* of the caller fails as before.

  A new attempt is a new SYN, and slirp sends it immediately. The harness counts the retries and shows them in the `--web-stress` details (`TLS handshake(s) repeated`).

  **Lost frames in an open connection (issue #117):** no connection retry covers these frames. slirp sends them again after approximately one second. This is sufficient to make a check with a 1 s limit fail. It occurred one time in the #106 check: a `GET` took 1.60 s, and the gateway logged `RX frame dropped` at that time.

  The harness measures each case of that check **one more time** only in one condition. The gateway log must have an `opencores.emac: … RX frame dropped` line in the window of that measurement. The window goes from the start to the end of the measurement, with a margin of 0.2 s / 0.5 s for the console latency. `results.json` records the discarded attempt (`remeasured`, with the line of the loss). The requirement of the check does not change: a failure without a loss in its window is a failure, and a second failure is also a failure.

  The project selected this solution, not 5 buffers. This solution does not change the firmware or the memory that #80 measures. Also, 6 buffers had already put the #80 margin at 1 KB from the threshold in a full run. With 5 buffers, frames would continue to be lost (4 → 30–58 per stress run, 6 → 0), so the check could still fail.

  This is the only network check with a limit of approximately 1 s. The other checks of ~1 s measure the Modbus polling (through the UART, not through the network). The harness does not limit the speed of its uploads, because the bursts also come from other simultaneous connections.
- **The "QEMU hang" in a *rollback* after the trial deadline (FW-1) was a firmware fault.** It occurred several times, also in the CI of `main` cf6fc7c (run 41875941691, `e2e-psram`). The output stops immediately after `secure_boot_v2: Take trusted digest key(s) from running app`, inside `esp_ota_mark_app_invalid_rollback_and_reboot()`. The deadline *rollback* ran in the `esp_timer` task (3.5 KB stack). The RSA-PSS verification of the previous image overflowed that stack. At that point, the E2E of `fix/ota-trial` printed `A stack overflow in task esp_timer has been detected`. Now the *rollback* runs in its own task (`fw_trial`, second round of #108 in `main`).

  The dump that this harness took confirms it. Core 0 was stopped in `esp_cpu_compare_and_set()`, called from `xQueueSemaphoreTake()` inside `esp_secure_boot_verify_sbv2_signature_block()`. The lock pointer was `0xffffffff` (overwritten memory), and core 1 was parked. For this reason, the harness no longer retries a silence. It also does not retry a scenario with a stack overflow or a different firmware failure on the console (§4.3).

  The harness continues to retry the faults that really come from the emulator. These are the QEMU SIGSEGV and `Bad ram pointer` faults with a loaded host. They also include the core 1 `LoadStorePIFAddrError` during the verification of a downloaded image (one time, in the second download of `GW42`).

  The same fault in the *tick* at the boot of a trial image occurred one time with *heap poisoning*. The harness no longer retries it: it counts as a firmware fault. If it occurs again, the `e2e-poison` job fails (a known emulator limitation in that job, refer to §4.3; the job does not hide it). It did not occur in the `--only ota` run with *heap poisoning* on the final tree (17/17). Nobody saw these faults outside QEMU, and there was no test on the board.
- **QEMU crashed with a loaded host (issue #107).** The symptoms were a SIGSEGV with no message, or `Bad ram pointer 0x4037…`/`0x4200…`. They occurred in the OTA scenarios, in the #97 scenarios and in the #99/#101 test, only with host load (a different E2E in parallel). There were 3 rounds of two parallel `--only ota` runs (MTTCG). In these rounds, 7 emulators died in 6 runs. 4 runs were affected, and 2 runs failed because of a second death in the same scenario. The host load was between 4 and 17 (in the last round, the minimal reproduction ran at the same time).

  With `-accel tcg,thread=single`, the same 3 rounds of two parallel `--only ota` runs gave **no QEMU death in 6 runs** (36 emulators). The host load was between 1 and 2.3. The cost is time. With two simultaneous runs, FW-1 OTA took 177–179 s (98 s in one full run alone with MTTCG). The #97 integration test took 78–82 s (55), and the #99/#101 test took 88–105 s (69).

  One of the 6 runs failed for a different reason. It was a silence in the image verification of the #99/#101 test (core 0 idle, core 1 in `park_loop`; refer to §6.10). The cause is not two emulators at the same time in the harness. The harness does not do that: the scenarios start one at a time. The cause is the host load.

  The QEMU core dumps show a race in its MMU model of the S3 with MTTCG. One vCPU is inside `esp32s3_write_mmu_value()` and reads the page again with `blk_pread()`, while the other vCPU executes. The other vCPU then crashes in the translated code, or when it looks for code (`Bad ram pointer`). A 40-line ESP-IDF application that only maps and unmaps flash pages on one core reproduces it. With a loaded host and the same map rate, it gave 3 crashes in 9 runs of 60 s with MTTCG, and 0 in 9 with `-accel tcg,thread=single` (`docs/upstream-reports.md` §7, with the text for espressif/qemu).

  For this reason, the harness runs the OTA scenarios with one thread, because they map the flash all the time (downloads, uploads, verifications). The other scenarios use MTTCG. The retry on QEMU death (#86/#90) stays for the faults that continue. One fault continued: in a run with PSRAM, QEMU aborted in `main` (MTTCG) with `malloc(): unsorted double linked list corrupted`. This occurred in a TLB *flush* during a restart, and the retry covered it.

  At a retry, the harness now moves the messages of the discarded attempt out of `CAPTURE` to `DISCARDED_CAPTURE`, which criterion 6 still decodes. The reason: the retry starts from the same gateway and repeated its bdSeq values, so SPB-2/SPB-11 counted two NDEATHs with the same bdSeq.

  The slow single-thread mode made it necessary to wait for evidence in three locations, with no change to what the checks examine. SPB-14 keeps each Voltage value until the slave responded to two reads. The harness waited up to 300 s for the response to a 1 MB web upload. With PSRAM and software AES and SHA, the upload took approximately 150 s, so with a loaded host the margin was small.

  **Issue #115:** now the harness waits while there is progress (`upload_firmware()`). Progress is the bytes that the gateway accepts (sent in pieces of 16 KB), and the upload and verification lines on the console. The wait fails after 90 s with no progress, or after 900 s in total (both scale with `--timeout-scale`). 90 s is more than the 60 s of silence that the harness already uses to detect a hang in the verification.

  `test_harness.py` tests this against a local TLS server. A slow upload with progress gets a response, a blocked upload fails in seconds, and the total limit stops an upload that does not end. The #102 check waits for the console line of the rejection, which arrived after the NDATA.
- **The park of core 1 blocked the verification of an image (issue #111, only the QEMU build).** `CONFIG_GW_FW_PARK_OTHER_CORE` (depends on `GW_IO_SIMULATED`) parks core 1 while the app maps the flash (§4.3). The `fw_park` task (priority 23) interrupted the task that ran on core 1. That task could be `rs485_bus`, which is pinned to that core, with a lock held. Then the verification task on core 0 blocked on that lock, and nothing released it. The symptoms were silence after `esp_image: segment 0`, core 0 idle, and core 1 in `park_loop`.

  **Which lock:** a natural reproduction occurred in 1 of 20 `--only ota` runs, two in parallel, with single-thread TCG, in `FW-1 OTA`. The gdb task dump showed `fw_update` in a wait for the `s_adjtime_lock` *mutex* of `gettimeofday()` (esp_libc `timekeeping.c`). The syslog copy of the log (`gw_log`, the time of the record) for the `segment 0` line requested this mutex. The owner of the mutex was `rs485_bus`, parked in `gettimeofday()` from `gw_poller_poll`. For this reason, that line came out and the next line did not.

  With the emulator command `storm` (the bus task floods the console), the hang occurs each time, with a different lock. It is the `stdout` lock, which `rs485_bus` holds in the middle of a line. The dump of the issue (from the #99/#101 test scenario) had no memory. It has the same signature, so it is almost certainly the same mechanism, with one of these two locks.

  **Fix:** before the park, the bus task stops between two transactions, where it holds no lock (`gw_bus_hold`, a wait of at least 10 s, see #112 below). If it does not stop, the park does not occur: `fw: core 1 park refused`. The IDF tasks pinned to core 1 (`IDLE1`, `ipc1`) do not take locks that the parked section needs. If `fw_park` interrupts a task with no affinity, core 0 continues that task and releases what it holds. No code of this project logs inside the parked section.

  A park lasts 45 s at most. After that, core 1 continues alone and the firmware logs `fw: core 1 park bound reached`. For the harness, this is a firmware failure, and the harness never retries it.

  **Check:** while the bus task floods the console, the E2E uploads an image through the web UI. The gateway verifies the image and rejects it. The upload responds 400, no core 1 lines occur inside any park, and the flood continues after the upload. Before the fix, the same check hung (POST with no response).

  **Issue #114:** with single-thread TCG (#107) and PSRAM, a flood during the full transfer made the 1 MB upload slower. It took more than 200 s of guest time, not approximately 25 s, and the `POST` (150 s) was cut. The upload continued, and the gateway rejected the next #98 download (`an update is already running`). Now the flood runs before each park. It runs from before the upload to its first park, and again from 80 % of the transfer (`Firmware Update/Progress`) to the response.

  The check also requires flood lines immediately before each of the two parks. The `POST` uses the progress wait of #115. The `storm` command accepts up to 600 000 ms. A final check requires that no park of the run is refused or gets to the limit.

  **Follow-up (issue #112):** (1) The bus task also stops between two attempts of one transaction (the `on_attempt` *hook* of the transport, with no locks held). The `gw_bus_hold()` wait comes from the RS485 configuration. It is the longest attempt plus 1 s, never less than 10 s. The longest attempt (`mb_master_attempt_max_ms()`) is the silence, the longest request at 12 bits per character, the response timeout and the frame timeouts.

  The slowest valid configuration is 1200 baud, 5 s, 5 retries and 1 s between frames. With it, the wait is approximately 11 s for each attempt. Before, the wait was the full transaction, which is more than 30 s. Thus no valid configuration causes `park refused` (host test `test_attempt_bound`). In the E2E, `GW47` with 5 s and 5 retries parks in the middle of a transaction, while `Meter1` does not respond. With the firmware of `main` 03544d0, the same scenario fails: the two parks of the upload end in `park refused` after 10 s.

  Issue #114: the harness waits up to 90 s, not 12 s, for the attempt after `Meter1`. If the transaction is already complete, the next request waits behind the tests of `Dead3..Dead5`, 5 s each.

  (2) An `esp_ipc_call()` or `esp_ipc_call_blocking()` to core 1 while core 1 is parked would wait until the end of the park. The reason: `ipc1` runs at the priority of the caller (`CONFIG_ESP_IPC_USES_CALLERS_PRIORITY`). If the caller holds a lock that the parked section needs, the system blocks. The QEMU build wraps the two calls at link time (`-Wl,--wrap`, in `gw_fw/CMakeLists.txt`). The wrapper records a call during the park with no log and no lock. Then `park(false)` reports it (`fw: blocking IPC to core 1 while it was parked`), and the harness counts it as a firmware failure.

  Today, `esp_intr_free()` (for an interrupt of the other core) and the other-core trace of `debug_helpers` use these calls. Neither call occurs in a parked section. Nobody tested the warning with a forced call. A check of the disassembly showed that the two calls go through the *wrappers*.

  **Limitations:** a new task pinned to core 1 must stop in the same way as the bus task. On the board, nothing changes: the option does not exist there.
- **A log line of a different task cut the `rollback stack` line (issue #123, only the QEMU build).** One full E2E run failed the #108 B4 check. The firmware was correct, but a part of an `edge:` line was inside the `fw: rollback stack: N B free in task fw_trial` line. The harness regex did not find the line.

  **Cause:** the *shutdown handler* `log_rollback_stack` wrote the line with `esp_rom_printf()`. This ROM function puts one character at a time into the UART FIFO and takes no console lock. The log lines of the other tasks hold the console lock of `gw_shell` and the `stdout` lock until the line ends. But a task can wait in the UART driver for space in the FIFO, with its line half written. The trial task can then write its full line into that half line. A task with a higher priority can also interrupt the ROM function.

  **Reproduction:** a temporary task on core 0 wrote lines of approximately 400 characters from the start of the rollback (priority 8, 1 tick between the lines). With the old code, `--only ota-fw1` gave `rollback stack: 2268 B free in task I (4600fw_trial …`, and the check failed. With the fix and the same task, the line was complete (2124 B free) and all 14 checks passed. The temporary task is not in the repository.

  **Fix:** the handler writes the line with `ESP_LOGW`, through the same locks. A line in progress ends first, and no log line or shell answer goes into it. The line now has the usual prefix (`W (ms) fw: rollback stack: …`). The harness checks did not change: they read the value and require at least 1 KB. The park rules of #111 stay correct, because no lock stays on core 1. The `esp_ota_ops` lines immediately before the handler use the same path.
- **RAM margin (#80):** since issue #135, the E2E measures it in QEMU with the octal PSRAM, the configuration of the board, with the harness configuration (5 devices, 74 tags). mbedTLS, the configuration and the engine are in PSRAM, so the margin of the internal RAM is large (§4.3). The thresholds did not change: the E2E fails below 8 KB free or 17 KB contiguous. A larger configuration, or more simultaneous HTTPS clients, decrease the margin. Before #135, the check ran in the QEMU build without PSRAM. There, approximately 14 KB stayed free at the lowest point, and approximately 37 KB since #91 (the print of the document no longer requests two times the text size).
- **TLS handshake during a validation (#84).** Before issue #135, the check ran without PSRAM. Now it runs in the PSRAM variant (the `tls` scenario). QEMU reproduced it in a deterministic way. The emulator command `validation hold <ms>` keeps the buffers of the next validation at their peak for 12 s (the second `gw_config_t` and its document). During that time, the harness cuts the connection to the TLS broker (a *relay*), with a different HTTPS session open.

  Result (node GW30): **the MQTT reconnection completes its TLS 1.3 *handshake* during the validation** (NBIRTH before the hold ends, one attempt only). The PUT responds 200, and the web session continues to get service. There is no restart, *panic* or *watchdog*. The minimum internal RAM was 14 352 B (the same as without the cut). The largest block at the release was 25 600 B.

  **No fix was necessary**, so the project selected none of the solutions of the issue. With the mbedTLS dynamic buffers (#80), a *handshake* does not reserve the 16 KB input buffer for the full session. A reconnection *replaces* the previous session (the cut releases its buffers), and does not add to it.

  A new HTTPS client during the validation would add memory, but this is already serialized. The web server has only one task, and the *handshake* of the new connection waits until the PUT ends. The opposite order is a PUT that arrives while a *handshake* is in progress. If no 32 KB block is available, the result is **503** `sin memoria para validar la configuración`, with no hang. This is the response of the #80 case before the dynamic buffers.

  The E2E does not force this order. A configuration larger than the harness configuration decreases the margin.
- `test_poll` (`test_unanswered_block_fails_the_cycle`) failed one time in 7 runs with `Expected …756 Was …757`. The timestamp of a tag that the firmware did not read changed by 1 ms. It seems to depend on the wall clock of the host (a millisecond change during the cycle). It does not come from the change that added the check, and nobody changed it.
- The host tests need network access the first time (FetchContent of Unity and cJSON).
- During an E2E run, a different process on the shared host used UDP 123 and stopped mosquitto by its name. For this reason, the harness uses a renamed copy of the broker and runs in its own *network namespace* (§4.3). Without isolation, if UDP 123 is in use, the TIME-1 check fails: it requires a response from the SNTP server of the harness.
- **pymodbus is pinned at 3.8.6**: version 3.16 changed the datastore API (SimDevice).
- `idf.py` does not accept `-j`: the build used the default ninja parallelism.
- **Old `sdkconfig` (#83):** the build check covers only the short list of §3.3. A different option that a person adds to `sdkconfig.defaults` later also does not get to an existing `sdkconfig`. If the firmware depends on that option, add it to `GW_SDKCONFIG_REQUIRED`. An intentional change in `menuconfig` to one of these options also makes the build fail (use `-D GW_SDKCONFIG_CHECK=warn`).
- There are two third-party components: nanopb (vendored) and `joltwallet/littlefs`. The project did not use the `livekit/nanopb` registry package, because it has an ABI defect. The project also did not use `espressif/esp_io_expander_tca9554`. That component loads the latch with 0xFF, which is OFF with active-low DOs. But it does not let the host tests examine the sequence or the polarity (`gw_dout`). The feasibility study (`docs/feasibility-idf.md`, IO-2) rejected it because it assumed, in error, active-high DOs.

### 6.11 Firmware (FW-1)

- **A new image that passes the self-check but cannot connect to the broker stays installed** (the accepted risk of #144, §3.6). The gateway does not see a difference between a broker that is down and a firmware that cannot connect. Do a manual rollback (upload the earlier image, or the button "Restart into `<version>`"). For fleets, update a pilot gateway first.
- **A power cut during the trial also causes a return to the previous firmware** (the bootloader cannot see a difference between a power cut and a *crash*). The record `fw_last` gives `rolled back from <v>: restarted during the test (power-on)`.
- **The self-check times (60 s, 120 s) are not measured on the board yet.** Issue #128 must measure the time of the self-check items and of a web upload, to confirm `CONFIG_GW_FW_SELFCHECK_S` and `CONFIG_GW_FW_BOOTLOOP_CLEAR_S`.
- **The boot-loop counter is in RTC memory that the bootloader does not reserve** (issue #149). The E2E shows that it survives the panic resets in QEMU. On the board, issue #149 must reserve it before the first flash.
- **The URL of an update has up to 255 characters**, through NCMD and through the web UI (issue #87). The texts of Sparkplug metrics can have up to 256 characters (`SPB_STRING_MAX`). The texts of properties and DataSet cells can have up to 63. The firmware rejects a longer text with a log, and never truncates it. Thus the firmware does not apply such an NCMD or DCMD, and does not publish such a message of its own.

  The `string` tags stay limited by their `length` (up to 16 registers, 32 characters), which the configuration validation checks. The `Firmware Update/State` status has up to 95 characters (`GW_NODE_FW_STATE_MAX`). The firmware publishes a longer status truncated, with a warning in the log. The full reason (up to 239 characters) stays in the shell (`status`) and in `GET /api/firmware`. This limit keeps the Sparkplug engine, a single allocation, in the 21 504 B *heap* class (issue #80). The cause in `fw_last` is cut to fit 95 characters; the log has the full path.

  The increase of the limit cost 193 B of stack per metric in the encoder and in the decoder (`Payload_Metric` of nanopb). The stack check of the Sparkplug task measured this (§4.3).
- **There is no version *anti-rollback*** (`CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_APP_ANTI_ROLLBACK`): the gateway accepts any valid image of the project that passes the pre-flight check, also an older one. The *anti-rollback* burns an eFuse counter, and the project burns no eFuse (§3.7).
- **An interrupted download does not continue:** it starts again. The status shows how many bytes arrived (`failed: download of <v> interrupted after N bytes`). The firmware reads the count before `esp_https_ota_abort()`, which releases the *handle*. Issue #98: before, the firmware read the count after the abort, from released memory. The E2E cuts a download at 300 000 bytes (§4.3). While the firmware writes the flash, the other tasks can see short delays (each erased sector stops the cache for some ms). The Modbus polling did not stop in the E2E, but nobody measured it on the board.
- A web upload runs in the `httpd` task and blocks the web UI until it ends.
- **During the trial of a new image, the gateway refuses configuration saves, updates and the other-slot button** (409). The trial is 60 s when the self-check passes, and at most 5 min.
- **If the firmware can never write `otadata`**, the image continues to run in trial, and the firmware tries again every 60 s. Saves and updates stay refused. A power cut causes a rollback. A planned restart also causes a rollback, after one last attempt (issue #99).
- **Flash writes in the `main` task.** The confirmation of an image (`esp_ota_mark_app_valid_cancel_rollback`, which maps `otadata` with `esp_partition_mmap`), the write of `fw_last` and of `lkg_net` occur one time, in the `main` task. A connection attempt writes nothing to the flash since issue #161 (the bdSeq is in RAM, §6.8). The rejection by the backstop timer writes `fw_last` from the `esp_timer` task, one time. These writes take some ms, far from the 15 s of the watchdog. They do not occur on the polling path or on the data publish path.
- **The gateway cannot install images older than the capability block (issue #102) when it has encrypted NVS.** This is also true when these images have NVS encryption, because they do not declare it. On a migrated gateway, OTA cannot go back to such a version.
- **Images older than #144 count as "unknown" for the pre-flight check.** They need force, and then they must reach recovery mode or better.
- **QEMU only:** an emulator hook (`/cfg/.sim-reject-config`, `CONFIG_GW_IO_SIMULATED`) makes a new image on trial reject the stored configuration (path `e2e.simulated`). The E2E uses it to examine the rejection of an image installed from normal mode, because all images that the E2E builds read the same schema.

### 6.12 No Secure Boot and no flash encryption (FW-2, FW-3)

The project does not use the eFuse-burning features (§3.7). Thus physical access to the board gives full control of it:

- A person with access to the USB-C port can read all of the flash. The flash contains the secrets in NVS (not encrypted), the files of LittleFS (the client key of MQTT-5, the certificates) and the *core dump*. If the secrets are important, put the gateway in a locked cabinet.
- That person can also flash a different firmware through USB. The owner can also do this, for example after a failed update.
- The default build does not verify a signature on OTA images. The gateway accepts each image of this project (`project_name`) with a correct checksum and SHA-256 (§3.6). The access control of the channel protects the update: the web password, the ACL of the broker for NCMD, and TLS with a server check for the HTTPS download.
- With PSRAM, the data in PSRAM is in clear text outside the chip (§6.5.1).
- `idf.py coredump-info` reads the *core dump* through USB. Without USB, the web API (with the password) and the shell give it (§3.2, issue #132).
